[REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — RULINGS-ROUND2 §E: the owner's doctrines of 2026-10-18 (verbatim on the kit's BOARD) read against this volume. THE WORLD HOSTS THE CAMPAIGN, NEVER COMPETES: the crown, its contests and its verdicts are setting and consequence, and no PL kind involves the party (§7). MACRO OVER MICRO: PL's justice is the feud between towns and the landed verdicts, never a trial. NO ENGINE MORTALITY CLOCK: a crown passes only through the landed ruling-power transfers and the ladder's seat changes; a ruler's death is the DM's, and his KILL card offers "ask the vassals" (STOP-2, §5.6); P17 stays DARK by design. MEDIEVAL FOR MILLENNIA: no era changes how a crown holds its vassals. MENACES NEVER EMERGENT and PURPOSE ONLY BY THE DM: PL births no menace and gives no court a destiny; a claim is a court's own decision read from the field (§5.2). No passage needed changing for them.]

**The owner's words (2026-10-17), verbatim.** "the physical world is being built now. The user is the interface between the party and the world. Think of this tool as the DMs tool not the party. the DM presents chohices and options to the party and then uses this tool to bring about consequences rather than the party directly. This is not a tool for the whole table. We only supply setting and consequence." — and, building the chair's idea of "the realm layer above settlements": "For this last one, it has to be based in the vassalage system and we also have to make it coherent and compatible with our FP per individual settlement. for example, a chain of vassalages and the one that sits at the very top is the capital, referencing a feudal like system. how do settlement relationships of a kingdom work between individual settlemetns vsersus a kingdom when it comes to matters of trade, war, alliance, etc.? Who takes precedents... are there nuances, are there comprimises,, etc. Does a civil war still mean its the same kingdom but despite that different settlemetns are organizing against each other? comprehensivley think it ou." (The owner's two later questions — conversion through the chain, and the crown's control of its vassals — head §14 and §15 verbatim.)

**The answer in one paragraph.** A kingdom is not a new thing in the world; it is the shape the vassal edges already make, read upward to the settlement that answers to no one. That settlement is the capital. Every town keeps its own court, its own beliefs and its own choices; being sworn adds one voice to every one of those choices — the liege's — and that voice grows band by band with the owner's own measure of the bond ("scales by how close the vassal is in culture and trade and relationship with the overlord"). The crown speaks abroad, each town rules at home, obligations bind the seam both ways, and how strong the crown's hold is decides how far its voice carries past its own vassals to theirs and whether its summons and its protection reach down the chain. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-1: "the town always keeps the larger share of its own decision" (owner-sounding prose for a chair ceiling) is struck; the liege is heard in every class at a band-mapped share and the hold sets the crown's reach (RULINGS-ROUND1 PL-r1-1).] Nothing is forbidden: a town may defy its liege, at a price, and two acts are the breaking of fealty itself. A feud is towns of one kingdom fighting their own quarrel; a break is a town leaving; a civil war is towns of one kingdom fighting over the crown — and yes, it is still the same kingdom, its towns on two sides. Faith, rites and the form of government drift down the chain slowly, over years; the crown's instruments of control act quickly and wear; and every one of them has a counterforce already in the world.

**Binding prior words.**
- ODQ §12 (owner): *"Nothing intrinsically makes a settlement a capital. The closest in-system analog is an overlord."* — the capital is DERIVED.
- `DESIGN_COHESION_WEAVE.md` §F.3b (owner refinement): an empire is *"A PATTERN, NEVER AN ENTITY … NAMING POLITIES IS DM TERRITORY … the engine builds the bones, THE DM BAPTIZES"*.
- ODQ §735.2 (owner): *"When it comes to an occupied or vassal settlement, the ruling power will have an equal or greater counterpart in all decision-makings that is derived from the overlord or occupier… When occupied, the occupier's influence is always just greater than the legitimate ruler … In vassalage, which is much more concrete, it can be lower than an occupier's, scales by how close the vassal is in culture and trade and relationship with the overlord, and is still influential."*
- ODQ §736.1 (owner): *"any attempted coup or rebellion has a scaling factor according to the legitimacy of the ruling powers."* · §741.1 (owner): *"…it needs to also consider what each foreign power — allied or neutral or enemy settlement — will react … regarding each decision as part of any process or major decision."*
- ODQ §934.87 (owner, the verbatim choice): *"Build real realm borders"*; the entry records the lens's source as "the seat read `foreignSeatOf`, its root sovereign, over the territory partition" (the recording seat's description, not the owner's words). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — HOST-r1-1: attribution corrected; PL-1 draws the borders lens from `polityOf` — the seat read restricted to MEMBER bases (PL1) — with the treaty-only sphere drawn apart (§9 PL-1).]
- Amendment I (owner): *"expand all of this to wars between allied kingdoms and to three-or-more-party wars"* → a multi-party war is *"A GRAPH OF BILATERAL RELATIONSHIPS"*. Amendment S (owner): *"originals and overlords may TRADE their satellite or vassalized settlements"*.
- W-SEAT laws (`DESIGN_W_SEAT.md` §2): ONE RESOLVER PER QUANTITY · DERIVED, NEVER PERSISTED, except where a DECISION is state · PRIMACY IS ORDERING, WEIGHT IS SCALAR · `foreignSeatEnabled` is OWNER-LIT (ODQ §820 Q-S3). [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M4: W-SEAT A1.2.15 (`foreignSeatOf`'s codepoint-first multiplicity, pinned in the seat suite) is AMENDED BY NAME here: PL12's greater-weight rule applies INSIDE `polityOf` only, so two tie rules live on two surfaces, both registered; `foreignSeatOf` and its four production readers are untouched.] [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-m4: the patron exclusion in `foreignSeatOf` is the chair's vetoable READING of §735.2, not an owner ruling.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-7: `polityOf` could weigh a second overlord only by re-deriving its grip (W-SEAT: ONE RESOLVER PER QUANTITY). Cured by ONE additive export `vassalageSeatsOf(worldState, snapshot, sid)` — every candidate view, codepoint-sorted, computed by the same private grip law — read by `foreignSeatOf` (its choice unchanged: the first) and by `polityOf` (the heaviest; codepoint on a tie). Under `polityPrecedenceEnabled` the member's foreign book and `liegeStanceToward` follow `polityOf`'s liege, so a sworn member has one liege on every polity surface; W-SEAT's coup term (`foreignSeatCoupAdj`) and its suitors keep codepoint-first — the two registered surfaces the A1.2.15 amendment names.]
- W-SEAT's deferred `civilContests` ("an owner-gated class no lane may take alone", `irregularForce.js`) is a contest INSIDE one town; the polity contest is BETWEEN towns of one polity — registered apart; this program's delegation (10-16, 10-17) is the authority for `polityContests` (SX-26).
- Spine requirement 10: "out-of-posture acts are priced and receipted news, never forbidden"; requirement 24: THE HEARING WEEK.

[CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — R4-13: three facts the draft's banner had wrong are corrected in place: (1) faith ALREADY flows along sworn links in the two spread presets (`faithCarriersOut`); (2) rite imposition is ONE trade per conquest-made vassalage, not a steady yearly flow; (3) `closeness01` ALREADY carries a faith term.]

---

## §1 THE LAWS (PL1–PL17)

**PL1 — THE POLITY IS A PATTERN, NEVER AN ENTITY.** A polity is the closure of SWORN links upward to a PARAMOUNT: the settlement whose own looming read names no overlord. Members, tiers, the paramount and every band are DERIVED per read; membership is never stored. THE CAPITAL IS THE PARAMOUNT — derived, never a field (ODQ §12). A sworn link is a `foreignSeatOf` view with `regime: 'vassalage'` whose `basis` is the vassal EDGE (`vassal_edge` · `vassal_edge_treaty_floor`) or the matured rung (`occupation_rung_vassalized`) ONLY; a `treaty_subordinating_ties` view (a tributary, a puppet seat) is the SPHERE, never membership. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-3 (RULINGS-ROUND1 PL-r1-3): `foreignSeatOf` step 3 returns `regime: 'vassalage'` for treaty ties alone, so the old predicate swallowed the sphere; the walk filters on basis and P34 pins it. An edgeless matured-rung member (the ladder topped out with no edge) is a member for the walk, precedence and the hearings; its edge-keyed records (§4.6, §5.3) wait on the edge.] [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M11: ONE pattern with two arms — the polity IS §F.3b's sworn-link arm; `hegemonyRead` keeps its treaty-only arm as the SPHERE; one christening ledger serves both (`.polity`, `.sphere`); `hegemonyFear` gains the polity as a second source of `fear_of_dominance` (§3.4).] A patron is influence, not sovereignty, and is never a member.

**PL2 — ONE ROOT READ ABOVE THE ONE SEAT READ.** `polityOf` walks `foreignSeatOf(…).patronSettlementId` upward with a cycle guard and a depth cap; it never re-derives who looms over a settlement (W-SEAT: one resolver per quantity). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-7: each step reads the one resolver — `foreignSeatOf` for occupation and the matured rung, `vassalageSeatsOf` for edges (the heaviest candidate, PL12) — never a second grip law.] [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M3 · SX-22: THE WALK STOPS AT ANY NON-VASSALAGE VIEW. An occupied lord is a paramount (or mid-lord) IN CHAINS: its own class is `occupied`, its sub-tree stays sworn to it, and the polity stands with a held head until each direct vassal answers `polity_fallen` (§5.7).] [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M7: the walk is memoized per snapshot (the `foreignPrimacy.js` per-pass idiom) with a cost pin (DRAFT ≤ 2 ms per tick at 14 settlements, UNMEASURED).] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PL-r1-6 (RULINGS-ROUND2 B: "the walk's cycle guard covers BOTH bases"): the guard sees a cycle whatever the bases of its steps. A cycle that closes only through a matured-rung step (`occupation_rung_vassalized`) with no sworn edge behind it is ONE LINK READ TWO WAYS. Executed (`cure/D-PL-r2-probe/cycle.rerun.out`): with H the overlord of M by the sworn edge and M holding H at the matured rung, `foreignSeatOf(H)` names M by `occupation_rung_vassalized` and `foreignSeatOf(M)` names H by `vassal_edge`. The walk follows the CONTRACT (the edge's stamps, PL16) and does not take the unbacked rung step: the rung's subject is a lord IN CHAINS — the root of its own polity, class `occupied` — and the polity stands whole (`cure/D-PL-r2-probe/walk.out`, worlds W1/W1b: from H, from M and from M's vassal L the walk names root H, with no receipt). Where a sworn edge backs the rung, `foreignSeatOf` itself reports `vassal_edge` (world W3), the step is an edge step, and a cycle of edge steps is PL12's circle (W3 yields `polity_circle`; an edgeless rung, W4, yields the lord in chains). Outside a contest the landed `vassalizationOutcomes` re-stamps an EXISTING occupier↔occupied edge at the rung's arrival ("Emit ONCE, on the tick the occupation first ARRIVES at vassalized"; with no edge, `if (!found) continue;` and the rung stays unbacked, W4); inside a contest `occupation.js`'s guard keeps the edge (§5.3), so the unbacked rung is the contest's case, and §5.1's "ONE polity for the contest's whole life" now holds through a conquest of the crown.]

**PL3 — THE ENGINE BUILDS THE BONES; THE DM NAMES THE KINGDOM.** The default display is descriptive ("Merewick and its sworn towns"); the engine never mints a polity name and NEVER MOVES ONE. The only persisted naming datum is the DM's christening, written only by the DM's `NAME_POLITY` act. [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M10: the engine never re-keys the name; at a `claim_prevails` verdict the card OFFERS the re-key as the DM's act; the default leaves the name on the old paramount's settlement as history and the polity under the new paramount unnamed until named.]

**PL4 — THE LIEGE'S VOICE IS IN EVERY DECISION, AT A SHARE THAT GROWS WITH THE OWNER'S SCALAR, AND NEVER ORDERS.** [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-B1 · SX-05: κ IS STRUCK.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-1 (RULINGS-ROUND1 PL-r1-1 replaces the SPHERE and the `0.45` clip): the sphere silenced the liege in whole classes keyed to the hold — a frayed liege was heard in no live decision (`warCoalitionLedger.js :: warCoalitionActive` needs `coalitionLedgerEnabled`, and the declaration reads no book) — and the clip flattened the owner's scaling above `strong`.] In every decision of a sworn member its immediate liege is a counterpart (§735.2: "an equal or greater counterpart in all decision-makings … scales by how close the vassal is in culture and trade and relationship with the overlord, and is still influential"). Its share is a MONOTONE BAND MAP of the owner's scalar `foreignSeatOf(…).weight01`, read through its landed band: `POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE = { marginal: 0.10, present: 0.25, strong: 0.38, dominant: 0.45 }` (DRAFT, UNMEASURED, FP-46 roster). So the voice scales band by band, and vassalage stays below the occupier's "always just greater" (§735.2: "it can be lower than an occupier's"). Wherever a decider reads the seat books, `keep = 1 − POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE[band]` for a sworn member; where a class's deciders read no book, the liege's voice enters through that class's landed seam (§3.2's second column). What the crown's HOLD governs is never which classes are heard, but (a) how far the PARAMOUNT's voice reaches past its own vassals (PL5) and (b) the obligation cascades (§4). Vassalage NEVER orders: primacy is occupation's alone, and the old "capped below an occupier's weight" clause stays dropped. PL mints NO loyalty quantity: a member's DEFERENCE is its seat weight and its word is the seat band. ⚑ OWNER-VISIBLE (OD-PL-12): the share map is the chair's reading of "an equal or greater counterpart … it can be lower than an occupier's", set beside the owner's quotation for his veto.

**PL5 — THE VASSAL OF MY VASSAL IS NOT MY VASSAL — EXCEPT AS FAR AS THE CROWN'S HOLD REACHES.** [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-B3 · SX-02: NO FIFTH BOOK.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-1 · PL-r1-18 · SEAMS-r1-12: the reach is a band map of the hold, not an `unbroken`-only switch; the head and the observer are named; ONE read serves CH and PT.] The paramount reaches a sub-vassal through the mid-lord's own decisions AND, in the sub-vassal's own decisions, through ONE blend inside the member's one foreign book (`foreignBookFor`): the stance term is `(1 − r)·liege + r·paramount` (`liegeStanceToward` read for each), where `r = POLITY_PARAMOUNT_REACH[band of polityHoldOf(worldState, snapshot, paramountId, subVassalId)]` — the paramount's hold over ITS direct vassals as the deciding sub-vassal believes it — with `POLITY_PARAMOUNT_REACH = { frayed: 0, shaky: 0.1, staunch: 0.2, unbroken: 0.3 }` (DRAFT, UNMEASURED) — liege homage. At `frayed` the paramount's share is zero and the partition is bit-identical to a liege-only book. The quantity is served once as `crownReachAt` (§2): CH's crown retainer, PT's crown backing and the crown's ban read it. Frozen `warTermination.js` takes zero lines.

**PL6 — THE CROWN SPEAKS ABROAD; THE TOWN RULES AT HOME; OBLIGATION BINDS THE SEAM; THE HOLD SETS HOW FAR THE CROWN REACHES DOWN THE CHAIN.** (The precedence law; §3 is its table.) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-1: the liege is heard in every class; the hold no longer counts classes.]

**PL7 — NOTHING IS FORBIDDEN; TWO ACTS ARE DEFINED, PRICED ON THE SPOT, AND HEARD.** A member's deciders remain its own; precedence is a weighted voice and receipted news (requirement 10). Two acts break fealty by definition: (a) a SEPARATE PEACE with its liege's enemy while the liege still fights it; (b) JOINING a war or coalition AGAINST its own liege. [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M15 · SX-23: the act is PRICED AT ONCE as defiance (a `fealty_defied` row in the fealty record and a deference patch through the one relationship writer) AND the break is proposed as the `fealty_break` hearing; a dismissal leaves the price. The detector is the polity stage (§0's host) reading this tick's new treaties and coalition joins. The remedy for a separate peace is the liege's war of submission, never a `quashed` roll. Act (b) is DARK wherever coalitions are dark (`coalitionLedgerEnabled` is false even in full_simulation) — PL-6's TIER line says so.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-10 · PL-r1-15: the definitional default reads THIS war's fealty rows — where the liege kept its relief (`relief_kept`) or failed its protection (`protection_failed`) on this edge since the war opened, the act is priced only and the hearing defaults `quashed`; else `succeeds` (§7) — so a losing crown that abandoned its members does not shed every member that sues; PL7's definitions, price and detector light under their own flag `polityBreakEnabled` (§8), never behind the owner-lit seat.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — the verifier's unnumbered minor (two PL7 brackets read as a contradiction): the 10-17 bracket's "never a `quashed` roll" means that the landed vassal rule's `vassal_rebellion_quashed` ROLL is never the remedy for a separate peace; the round-1 default `quashed` on the `fealty_break` card is a HEARING verdict read from this war's fealty rows. They are two different things and both stand. RULINGS-ROUND2 A.14 · C.Q3: these two acts are the ONLY triggers of a break. HARBOURING the crown's enemy — a member admitting a paragon under the crown's ban (CH §11) or granting passage to a unit whose mover is at war with its liege (PS §7.7) — is PRICED as a `fealty_defied` row with the deference patch, through PL's request door (§4.6), and NEVER proposes a break: the detector reads only this tick's new treaties and coalition joins (§0's host) and never the fealty record, so no `fealty_defied` row of any occasion can open `fealty_break`.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — the PL-r3-verify minor ("a `sent` followed by `falls` now defaults the break to `quashed`. P5 should say the siege did not fall"): under the two facts (§4.3, §4.9) a liege that SENT and still saw its member's walls fall has FAILED its protection, so a separate peace sued from that ruin is excused like an abandonment — the default `quashed` — and it reads the OUTCOME row, never the answer alone. The definitional default therefore reads, on this edge since the war opened, `relief_kept` (the liege refused) OR `protection_failed` (the member did not stand, whatever the answer), and P5's world states that M's siege held. A `peace_unkept` row (§4.8) is the MEMBER's default, not the liege's, and never excuses anything.]

**PL8 — OBLIGATIONS RUN BOTH WAYS, ARE ANSWERED, AND ARE REMEMBERED IN THE FEALTY RECORD.** Up: SERVICE, DUES, AID. Down: PROTECTION, ARBITRATION, PASSAGE. An up-obligation is answered by the member's own fork; a down-obligation is a DECIDED act of the liege. [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-B5 · SX-01: keeping and failing are written to `relationshipState.fealtyRecord`, a bounded archive on the sworn edge in the `allianceCalls` idiom (§4.6), never as turning points (the turning-point filter admits label changes only and erases anything else).] Protection kept is how deference is earned: by the liege's ANSWER at once (its patch — resentment, a grip term, rises on `kept`; trust, which steadies the vassal rule's stable compact and is no grip term, rises on `sent`) and by the OUTCOME over the year (the hold's `P`); §4.9 says what moves what, once. There is no second loyalty law. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — the PL-r3-verify minor ("PL8's base text still says 'through the trust/resentment patch that moves grip', which its own r3 bracket refutes"): the base text now says what the r3 bracket measured.] The crown's riders are the banner's (§4.7): a levied member's roads covered by the kingdom's net are the down-obligation of PROTECTION in a kingdom, recorded as a deed; keeping the peace in one's own land is part of SERVICE (§4.8). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PL-V5 (RULINGS-ROUND3 A.6): the record keeps TWO facts of every protection — the liege's ANSWER (`relief_sent` · `relief_kept`, which carries the patch: trust up on `sent`, resentment up on `kept`) and the OUTCOME (`protection_kept` · `protection_failed`, which carries no patch and moves the hold's `P`). Measured at master, resentment is a grip term and trust is not (§3.5's `P` bracket), so in the engine's own terms a liege that KEEPS its men home loses deference at once, one that SENDS steadies the oath's compact, and whether the member actually stood moves the hold.]

**PL9 — FEUD, BREAK, POLITY WAR: THREE DIFFERENT THINGS, AND A PAIR IS NEVER A WAR.** A FEUD is members at war over their own quarrel; a BREAK is a member leaving its liege; a POLITY WAR ("civil war" in prose) is members at war over THE CROWN ITSELF — every sworn link stays sworn in name, the polity stands, its members take SIDES, and the contest is the one persisted decision. [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M8: a polity of two is a PAIR — a claim inside it is a BREAK (the rebellion road), never a polity war; the claim path requires three or more members.]

**PL10 — FEALTY IS ANSWERED AT EVERY SEAT CHANGE OF A LORD, BY PL'S OWN DECIDER.** [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M2 · SX-14: the fealty question is PL's own decider (`fealtyQuestion`), not GR-4 extended; GRAMMAR keeps the treaty question (`succession_reaffirmation`), fold §3.1.10 amended to say so (the chair's).] Its producers, what each answer writes, and the one-hearing-per-pair rule are §5.6.

**PL11 — A FALLEN LORD NEITHER TAKES ITS VASSALS WITH IT NOR LEAVES THEM BLIND.** When a paramount or mid-lord falls to conquest, each direct vassal answers `polity_fallen`: `submit` · `stand_by` · `free` (§5.7). [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M3 · SX-22: `remnant` (which names the landed faction `legitimateRemnantOf`) is replaced by `stand_by`.]

**PL12 — ONE LIEGE, NO CIRCLE.** A member has exactly one liege in the polity read: where two overlord links exist, the greater seat weight, codepoint only on a tie — INSIDE `polityOf` ONLY, behind `polityEnabled` [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M4: the per-tick vassal rules keep serving every overlord edge as landed; multiplicity is rare by genesis; the lesser overlord keeps its dues and duties and is simply not the liege the walk names] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-7: the weights come from `vassalageSeatsOf` (one resolver, every candidate view); under `polityPrecedenceEnabled` the member's book and stance follow this liege (§0's W-SEAT bracket), so a member never has a heavier liege in the polity read and a codepoint-first one in its book; P3 pins `foreignSeatOf` byte-identical]. A circle is never a polity: the walk stops at the first repeat and receipts `polity_circle`; a subjugation that would close a circle is NAMED in its proposal (a suggestive guard — the DM decides). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PL-r1-6: "a circle" is an ALL-EDGE cycle; a cycle that closes only through an unbacked matured-rung step is one link read two ways and resolves to a lord in chains (PL2), never a `polity_circle`.]

**PL13 — EVERY POLITY ENGAGEMENT IS HEARD, ON THE WEEK.** Each kind in §7 is a registered engagement (requirement 24): minted only at the DM's one-week advance, alive that one week, resolved first at the next advance from the DM's answer or the default; month, season and year advances run defaults; nothing queues. The polity war is STANDING.

**PL14 — THE MOUNT RULE.** Zero lines in `pulseKernel.js` (1581), `applyWorldPulse.js` (907), `warTermination.js` (818), all frozen. Hosts at the ceiling take nothing without a leaf-first car: `generosityKernel.js` 800, `peaceTerms.js` 799, `armyTransitKernel.js` 798, `roadsKernel.js` 838 (baselined), and every host above 740 (`factionCompetition.js` 775, `convergence.js` 764, `settlementStrategy.js` 763, `relationshipRulesCore.js` 744) takes ONE call into a leaf, never logic. The POLITY STAGE is a leaf called from `dispositionChannels.js :: advanceTreatiesWithDisposition` (55 lines) [REFINED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — the draft named no per-tick host (SX-04's class of defect); the treaty-advance wrapper is the measured home: it runs after this tick's treaties, holds snapshot, worldState, graph and tick, and returns worldState and news the kernel already reads]. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-14 · PL-r1-2: "ONE call into a leaf" is kept per HOST, not per wave — each host above 740 takes ONE polity hook returning a bundle its sites read (§9's HOOKS table, measured once at the first wave's car-0 against 800); the verdict's relabels are minted PRE-FOLD by one call from `coup.js :: coupVerdictOutcomes` (126) into `src/domain/worldPulse/polityVerdict.js` and applied by the frozen applier (§5.5) — the polity stage itself writes no label.]

**PL15 — THE PHYSICAL WORLD IS A SEAM, NOT A PREMISE.** The borders' geometry and distance from the capital read the physical world when it lands (`mappedDistanceWeight`, aspatial = 1). Every PL wave has an aspatial arm and is whole without it. [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — the chair's host ruling (1) (BOARD 00:41): the concrete carrier is the map module's world canon — `mappedDistanceWeight` reads the digest the host's H5 rebuilds (HOST-r1 §1: AGREES); PL-1's borders lens waits on M-W2/H13 for the map's own surface and draws on the host's interim map layer until H19 re-points it (§9 PL-1).]

**PL16 — BELIEF, NEVER TRUTH, EXCEPT THE CONTRACT.** [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M5 · SX-21: every observer reads the polity's state — strength, sieges, the hold — through its own belief (K3; truth only in the omniscient preset); a lord reads its own sworn CONTRACTS true (the landed carve-out: "the belief fogs their STATE, not the contract"). The hold is therefore per observer, never one band per polity.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-8: the belief map holds no third party's label or war state and no believed legitimacy (`beliefMap.js`: records `{ readiness, strengthBand, allianceLabel, faithLabel, confidence01, lastUpdateTick }`; the label observer↔subject only). RULED BY NAME on the `readBeliefRelationship` precedent ("the declared relationship is public"): a liege's DECLARED label and open war toward a counterparty, and a court's `publicLegitimacy`, are PUBLIC truth for every observer; STRENGTH is believed (`readBeliefStrength`); the CONTRACT — the sworn edges and their seat weights — is truth for the polity's own lord and members (the landed carve-out widened from the lord to its sworn towns); a FEALTY RECORD is known to the two courts on its edge only. No new belief axis, no new persisted shape.]

**PL17 — CONVERSION IS SLOW; INSTRUMENTS ARE FAST AND WEAR.** [REFINED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — R4-14, R4-12: the owner's "these conversions that happen are slow" is BUILT, not tuned: every conversion draw in §14 is keyed by YEAR (the rite idiom `…:${sid}:${year}`) and every constant is named on `INTERVAL_WEEKS`; every control instrument in §15 is a typed decaying incident on the sworn edge (the memory weave's idiom), one decay law for all.]

## §2 THE ONTOLOGY

**Member classes** (derived; closed `POLITY_CLASSES`): `paramount` — answers to no overlord and has at least one sworn member · `vassal` — sworn to a liege, at tier 1..n (the landed word in its landed meaning) · `occupied` — held below `vassalized` by a member (the occupier's primacy governs it; it counts toward the polity's lands and strength, not its hold), or a lord in chains (PL2). [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SX-16 · SPL-m1: `root · sworn · held` are struck as values (`root` generic in 205 files, `sworn` = `treaty.sworn`, `held` three landed meanings); `paramount` is free (0 src · 0 tests · 0 docs); `rootId` and `liegeId` live only as compound fields.] Prose says "the capital", "sworn to", "held by".

**The reads** (pure, lazy, memoized per snapshot, never persisted, outside the first-paint closure):
- `polityOf(worldState, snapshot, id)` → `{ rootId, liegeId, tier, path: [id … rootId], polityClass, basis, members? }` (members only when asked for the paramount; `basis` = the member link's `foreignSeatOf` basis). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — HOST-r1-1: this tuple is what the host sends per seat in its political window (H13) as a value "by the host's law" (§9 PL-1), so the map has the polity to draw as a host fact and need not re-derive it.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — HOST-r2-4: "so the map never re-derives the polity" read as a supersession of the map's own derivation, which it is not. The tuple is an OFFER, additive beside H13's accepted raw `PolityLink` rows: the HOST's borders lens draws it as a host fact (the host document's law 10: "facts recomputed by the host's own exported function on the same snapshot, labelled so"), while the map's SWAY stays one of "the two owner-ruled map derivations, the sway and the patrol cadence, drawn as the map's own" (HR:671), and "Two partitions, never one" (HR:499) stands. The map MAY adopt the tuple for its sway's member links; that is the map program's choice (§9 PL-1 (c), the text of record).] `politiesOf(worldState, snapshot)` → every polity with ≥ 2 settlements, codepoint-sorted.
- `polityHoldOf(worldState, snapshot, rootId, observerId)` → `{ hold01, band, terms }` — the crown's hold as `observerId` believes it (§3.5's DRAFT formula).
- `liegeStanceToward(worldState, snapshot, memberId, counterpartyId)` → `{ liegeId, label, atWar, stance: 'for' | 'against' | 'none' }` — the liege's own DECLARED label and open war toward the counterparty, PUBLIC truth by name (PL16). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-8: "read through the member's belief" struck — the belief map holds no third party's label.] The liege is `polityOf`'s (PL12).
- `swornLinkSuspended(worldState, snapshot, edge)` → boolean — true while a polity contest stands and the edge's two ends stand on OPPOSITE sides: one on side `crown` (the contested lord itself is always `crown`) and the other on side `claimant` (the claimant itself is always `claimant`); `aside` never suspends; an edge between two members of one side is never suspended (§5.3). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-16: the old disjunction suspended every edge touching the crown or the claimant, so a loyal member's link to its own crown read `rival`.]
- `crownReachAt(worldState, snapshot, rootId, memberId, observerId)` → `{ share01, liegeId, tier }` — the paramount's share in a member's decisions: `1` for the paramount itself; `POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE[band]` of the member's own seat weight for a DIRECT vassal; `POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE[band toward its immediate liege] × POLITY_PARAMOUNT_REACH[band of polityHoldOf(worldState, snapshot, rootId, observerId)]` for a sub-vassal at any depth (one blend, never compounded; `0` under a `frayed` hold). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — the verifier's unnumbered minor (`observerId` went unused by the formula): the hold is read as the OBSERVER believes it; a consumer deciding AT the member passes `observerId = memberId` (PL5's case, P37's "as L believes it", CH's member-side ban); the signature CH and PT cite is unchanged.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — SEAMS-r1-12: ONE read for the crown's reach — CH's crown-bound lands are the members where `share01 > 0`, and PT's crown backing weight and the crown's ban read the same quantity (each consumer's own threshold stays its volume's).]
- `vassalageSeatsOf(worldState, snapshot, id)` — W-SEAT's module, ONE additive export: every vassal-edge candidate view for `id`, codepoint-sorted, by the one private grip law (PL-r1-7; §0's W-SEAT bracket).
- `seatEpisodeKeyOf(worldState, settlementId)` → string — the seat's EPISODE KEY (§4.6): an FNV hash of `rulingPower.js :: authorityTransferEpochFor(settlement)` joined to the id of the settlement's newest `seatTransitions` row. It changes exactly when either landed tail gains a row, tick-less rows included. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PL-V3: §4.6's key, named and exported for the shared read below.] [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — RULINGS-ROUND3 A.14 ("`hashInt` → the landed `hash01` / `fnv1a32`"): PL minted no `hashInt`, no `a␟b` and no `cut01`; its "FNV hash" here and in §4.6 is the landed `kernel/proseHash.js :: fnv1a32` (`export function fnv1a32(str) {`), named so no builder mints a second.]
- `seatChangedSince(worldState, settlementId, since)` → `{ changed, via: 'transfer' | 'seat' | null, atTick }` — THE ONE SHARED READ of "a seat changed hands" (RULINGS-ROUND1 ADDENDUM SEAMS-r1-3; fold §3.1.10 ↳), over BOTH landed tails: `powerStructure.previousGovernments` (`transferRulingPower`'s rows — coup verdicts, conquest, appointment, the DM's `CHANGE_RULING_POWER`) and the ladder record's `seatTransitions` (coup · challenge · succession · vacancy · government_change). `since` takes two forms. (1) AN EPISODE KEY (a string from `seatEpisodeKeyOf`, recorded by the consumer at its own mint): `changed` ⇔ the current key differs — EXACT, every row of both tails counted. PL's fealty question reads this form (§5.6: the vassal's newest epoch-bearing row carries the lord's key). (2) A TICK (a number): `changed` ⇔ a row of either tail is stamped after `since`. A tick-less transfer row cannot be placed by a tick — only the DM's own `CHANGE_RULING_POWER` writes one (`mutateWorld.js :: changeRulingPower` passes `{ cause }` alone; `transferRulingPower`: `const tick = … (Number.isFinite(opts.tick) ? opts.tick : null)`) — so it counts only when it stands LATER in the tail than a row stamped after `since` (it is newer than that row); otherwise it reads as no change, and a consumer that must catch it records the key at its mint (form 1) or keeps its DM card's option (STOP-2's precedent: the DM authored that change). The ladder's organic rows are written only where the war layer and war termination are lit (`npcLadderKernel.js`: `const warRulingsLit = … warLayerEnabled === true && … warTerminationEnabled === true`), and an applied `government_change` writes one too (`applyWorldPulse.js`'s `applied:<outcome id>` rows); elsewhere the transfer tail alone answers. CH's pardon at a new court, PT's `backing_repudiated` and GR's successors consume it; pure, derived, never persisted; no gate of its own (each consumer's flag gates its use); built in PL-0's module or by the first consumer to land (the shared-leaf idiom). P39 pins both forms. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PL-V3: the ADDENDUM's read was registered by the fold (§5's PL round-1 token row) and consumed by PT (§7.3, §9.3, PT-3) and CH (CH-5), yet absent here — the lane ran before the ADDENDUM.]
- THE BANNER STACK (RULINGS-ROUND3 §F.1) is NO NEW READ: a patrol's stack is `polityOf(worldState, snapshot, homeId).path` (home → … → paramount) read rung by rung with `crownReachAt` at each rung, and CUT at the first rung whose link `swornLinkSuspended` reads true (§4.7 F.5); derived per read, never stored. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — A.7 · §F.1: the derived stack named over the two landed-in-design reads, so THE PATROLS and THE PASSAGE cite one chain.]
- `huntOrderOf(worldState, snapshot, { liegeId, memberId, bodyId })` → `{ ordered, reasons }` — the liege's ORDER that its member hunt a body in its land (§4.8): pure, draw-free (the `reliefAnswerOf` idiom), read by THE BODIES' `body_stance` fork as one input line on the member's card. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — THE BODIES' route (3), `cure/L-BD.md` §4; the name is free on master (0 · 0, `cure/D-PL-r4-probe/measure.out`).]

**The hold** (per observer; compound field `polityHold`; band words `frayed · shaky · staunch · unbroken`, lowest to highest). [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SX-16 · R4-9: `cohesion` is landed three times (`LAW_BAND_KEYS`, faction cohesion, cartography); `nominal · loose · firm · absolute` all collide (`'nominal'` npcGenerator, `firm` PULL_BANDS rung, `loose` pulseKernel, `'absolute'` CSS).] [REFINED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — the chair's fourth word `sound` is LANDED (`houseActs.js`: `'overdrawn', 'tight', 'sound', 'flush'`); `staunch` is free (0 · 0 · 0) and takes its place.]

**Deference** (per member) = the seat weight and band (PL4); prose: a `dominant` member "weighs Merewick's word in all it does"; `marginal`, "owes Merewick little but the name". [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-1: "answers to Merewick in all things" overstated a share of at most `0.45`.]

**The crown** = the paramount's court in its polity role: one court, two hats, the same deciders. The engine mints no separate actor.

**The polity's prose noun** follows the paramount's ruling power (one closed prose table, never a token): autocrat → kingdom, "the crown of X" · council → commonwealth, "X's council" · theocracy → see, "the see of X" · merchant_league → league, "the league of X" · criminal → ring, "X's ring" · mixed → "X and its sworn towns". [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — HOST-r1-5: "sway" is the map program's projection (its sway lens, H19's "allegiance" lens), so the criminal noun is "ring" (prose only; the one quoted `'ring'` in `src` is a town-map anchor glyph, another surface).] "Realm" is the campaign world's word and is never used in FP prose (the map program says "realm" for a polity shape and for the DM's realm names on ITS surfaces — a cross-surface homonym, registered); `polity` stays out of DM-visible prose (the institutional catalog already says "an independent polity", SPL-m6).

**Precedence classes** (closed `PRECEDENCE_CLASSES`): `war_abroad · peace_abroad · alliance_abroad · diplomacy_abroad · trade_abroad · justice_at_home · own_seat · fealty_itself` (§3). [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — the owner's PASSAGE word (BOARD 00:47: foreign military units moving through a kingdom's or a settlement's territory) is THE PASSAGE volume's (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PS.md`, compiled in round 2); PL's part — a passage class row (its token by PS's census), the liege's protection of a trespassed member, and the price on a member that harbours its crown's enemy's column — lands as PS's coupling rows; the chair adds the class at P4.]

## §3 PRECEDENCE — WHO DECIDES WHAT (the owner's question, answered class by class)

The rule beneath every row: **the member decides; its immediate liege is one voice in EVERY class, at `POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE[band]` of the owner's scalar (PL4); the paramount reaches past its own vassals as far as the crown's hold carries it (PL5).** Nothing here is a veto. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-1: the SPHERE (which classes hear the liege) and the `0.45` clip are replaced by the band map and the hold's reach (RULINGS-ROUND1 PL-r1-1).] [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-B1 · SX-05: the κ column is replaced by a SPHERE column per hold band; SPL-m3: every DRAFT cell is marked UNMEASURED.]

**3.1 Whose hold, at each site.** [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-18: every site names its head and its observer; the 10-17 bracket that keyed the SPHERE to the immediate liege's hold is superseded with the sphere.] Every lord is the head of its own sub-tree for this read. (a) The paramount's reach into a sub-vassal's book (PL5, `crownReachAt`): `polityHoldOf(worldState, snapshot, paramountId, subVassalId)` — the paramount's hold over its direct vassals as the deciding sub-vassal believes it. (b) The summons cascade and the direct summons (§4.1): `polityHoldOf(…, crownId, crownId)` — the crown's own read decides how far it summons. (c) The paramount's protection reaching a sub-vassal (§4.3): `polityHoldOf(…, paramountId, paramountId)`. (d) A liege's ORDERS — to end a feud and its war of submission (§6), and to hunt a body in a member's land (§4.8) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — THE BODIES' route (3)]: `polityHoldOf(…, liegeId, liegeId)`. (e) The crown's riders quartered on members' roads (§15.12), the crown's NET and the banner's protection occasion (§4.7 F.2, F.4) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — A.7]: `polityHoldOf(…, rootId, rootId)` — the root's own read decides whether it quarters them, how it composes its net and whether it owes a levied member the net's cover, and whether a foreign sponsor's hand in a member's land affronts the crown (§4.8 (5)) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — A.8]. (f) The RIGHT OF THE BANNER at a member's border — whether a fellow member's riders ride its roads by right or cross on THE PASSAGE's ladder (§4.7 F.3; PS's jurisdiction read cites this site): `polityHoldOf(…, rootId, holderId)` — the receiving member decides at its own border on its own belief (PL5's case: "a consumer deciding AT the member passes `observerId = memberId`") [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — A.7 · §F.3]. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — the PL-r2-verify minor ("§15.12 cites '§3.1 b' for the riders' quartering, but §3.1 (b) is the summons"): the quartering's own site is (e), and §15.12 cites it.]

**3.2 THE PRECEDENCE TABLE** (the liege's voice is present in every class at `POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE[band]`; the paramount's reach `r` by hold is `frayed 0 · shaky 0.1 · staunch 0.2 · unbroken 0.3`; every constant DRAFT, UNMEASURED, FP-46 roster). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-1: the table becomes class → the liege's voice (always, and the seam that carries it) · the paramount's reach by hold · what it means in play.]

| Class (`PRECEDENCE_CLASSES`) | The liege's voice — always present, and its seam | The paramount's reach past its own vassals (by hold) | What it means in play |
|---|---|---|---|
| `war_abroad` — declaring or joining a war on an outsider | the seat book at `warCoalitionDecision.js` · `warCoalitionRefusal.js`; the declaration through the chooser's shared-signal car (§3.4) | the stance blend `r` in a sub-vassal's one book (PL5) | A sworn town that marches on a neighbour its liege is at peace with is pulled back by its liege's voice — a little at `marginal`, hard at `dominant`. |
| `peace_abroad` — making or accepting peace with an outsider | the book at `warPeaceDecision.js` · `peaceTermsCoalition.js` and frozen `warTermination.js` (the default class) | the blend `r` | When the crown makes peace with Kell, each member at war with Kell weighs it on its own front (Amendment I: a graph of bilateral peaces); a SEPARATE peace while the liege still fights is PL7(a). |
| `alliance_abroad` — `defensive_pact`, `allied` with an outsider | the book at the pact answer (`pactFormation.js`, the one new consumer) | the blend `r` | A pact with the liege's enemy is weighed against the liege's stance and its anticipated reaction (§741.1). |
| `diplomacy_abroad` — envoys and offers to outsiders | the book at the two envoy reads (interception, the negotiation picture) | the blend `r` | The crown's quarrel colours how a sworn town receives a stranger's envoy. |
| `trade_abroad` — trade pacts and stances toward outsiders | no decider reads the book; the liege's EMBARGO is one input to the member's EC-6 stance at a named rung below the pair's own war stance and above the trade-memory rung, carrying the liege's share (DRAFT; EC's volume takes the bracket — the chair's) | the paramount's embargo reaches a sub-vassal at `r` | When the crown embargoes Kell, its sworn towns' trade with Kell cools in step with their deference. |
| `justice_at_home` — a feud between members; and THE KING'S PEACE, a body (a gang, a beast, a conclave — THE BODIES) in a member's land [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — THE BODIES' route (3): the member's `body_stance` is a decision of a sworn member, so PL4 puts the liege's voice in it; one class, widened, no ninth class] | the common-liege mediator rule in EVERY band (§6); for a body, the liege's own word toward it as an input of the member's `body_stance` fork at `POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE[band]` (§4.8) | the liege's order to end a feud, or to HUNT a body, at `staunch`+ by its own read (§3.1 d); under `unbroken`, a refused feud order is cause for the war of submission, while a refused hunt order is priced only (harbouring, §4.8) | §6; §4.8. |
| `own_seat` — coups, councils, faith at home | the landed W-SEAT coup term (`foreignSeatCoupAdj`: the liege defends the government it deals with), `preserve_order` (§15.2), recognition (§15.1) | none — backing is landed one hop | A liege backs its member's incumbent and never a challenger in its own member. |
| `fealty_itself` — changing liege, breaking free, being conveyed | the member's DEFERENCE (its seat weight) inside PL's own deciders: the fealty question, the allegiance choice, the break (§5) | none | The landed break road (heard, §7); WR-10's sale of a vassal is the LIEGE's own act in this class. |

**3.3 The liege's stance — the term that sees the counterparty.** [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-B2: the foreign book's term was the liege ruler's temperament (`rulerBiases`), blind to the opponent.] `liegeStanceToward` becomes the foreign book's DIRECTIONAL term for sworn members, composed inside `warSeatBooks.js :: foreignBookFor` (host 492; its caller `readWarSeatBooks` already holds `opponentId`, so the module-private function gains the argument): at peace with the counterparty → the book leans against war with it (its `continueBias01` toward peace); at war with it → for war; no relation → the temperament term alone. The blend is declared once (DRAFT `0.6 stance / 0.4 temperament`, UNMEASURED). The share binds at the one place it can: `keep = 1 − POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE[band]` for a MEMBER view (PL1's bases) only — occupation and the treaty-only sphere keep W-SEAT's landed `keep = 1 - foreignWeight01`. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-1: the `0.45` ceiling is replaced by the band map.] Everything sits behind `foreignBookFor`'s landed gate (`foreignSeatEnabled !== true → null`), so PL-3 waits on the owner's lighting (OD-PL-5). The crown's embargo enters EC-6's stance derivation at a new rung BELOW the pair's own war stance and ABOVE the trade-memory rung (DRAFT; EC's volume takes the bracket — the chair's).

**3.4 The ten book call sites, classed** (`rg 'readWarSeatBooks('`, CONFIRMED). `readWarSeatBooks` takes `decisionClass`; absent ⇒ `peace_abroad`, because the two callers that cannot pass an argument are frozen `warTermination.js`'s, and they ARE the termination decision. [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-B2's unclassed sites.] [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — with the sphere struck (PL-r1-1) the class no longer decides WHETHER the book is read; it carries the two exemptions (`fealty_itself` at the sale, `exempt` at army transit) and the autonomy charter's named classes (`narrowed_voice`, §3 compromises).]

|---|---|

| `warPeaceDecision.js` · `peaceTermsCoalition.js` | `peace_abroad` |

| `armyTransitKernel.js` (798/800) | EXEMPT — transit, not a decision: `decisionClass: 'exempt'` rides INLINE on the existing call line (zero effective lines; measured at PL-3's car-0, else the default stands and is recorded) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PS-r2-25: "passage" is THE PASSAGE's word for a unit crossing a holder's territory (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PS.md`, the owner's word of 10-18), so an army's march along its path is TRANSIT here; in PL, "passage" names only the charter's toll right (PL8, §4.5); the fold §5 register row is the chair's] |

The DECLARATION of war reads no book today (zero `readWarSeatBooks`/`foreignSeatOf` calls in `settlementStrategy.js`, `warIntent.js`, `warDeployment.js`, `pactFormation.js`, `tradeWar.js`). It reads the book through W-SEAT D2's owed chooser seat term, built on THE CHOOSER'S SHARED-SIGNAL CAR (SX-08: one context read in `settlementStrategy.js` that EC-17, MN-4, PL-4, HB row 7 and INT's factor seam all consume; built by the first of those chains to land; the fold records it as a cross-program unit). PL-3 names that car as its car-0 and waits on it. The pact answer (`pactFormation.js`, 565) reads the book for `alliance_abroad` through one call — ten landed call sites plus this one new consumer. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-24.]

**3.5 The hold — the DRAFT formula** (every weight and threshold UNMEASURED, FP-46 roster). [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M5 · SX-21; REFINED by R4-6 and the chair's §6 ruling: the mean seat weight already carries grip (leverage, dependency, pact, fear, resentment) and closeness (faith axes, rites, government, the trade label); adding resentment, burden or fault lines again would double-count.]

`hold01(head, observer) = clamp01( 0.45·D̄ + 0.20·L + 0.20·Sᵦ + 0.15·P − 0.15·G − 0.10·X )`
- `D̄` — mean `foreignSeatOf(member).weight01` over the head's DIRECT vassals (landed) — the CONTRACT read, truth for the lord and its members (PL16).
- `L` — the head's `publicLegitimacy.score / 100`, PUBLIC truth by name (PL16). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-8 · PL-r1-21: "as the observer believes it" is struck (no believed-legitimacy read exists), and so is "recognition given lands HERE" — recognition writes the MEMBER's legitimacy (§15.1), never the head's.]
- `Sᵦ` — the head's BELIEVED strength share among head + direct vassals (the head reads its contracts true and its vassals' strength believed): each strength is `readBeliefStrength(observerId, id, worldState, settlementStrength(item))` over `relationshipRuleHelpers.js :: settlementStrength`, the one comparator the vassal rules already read and the host's political window sends as the strength order. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — HOST-r1-15: the read is named; the map's FORCE band and garrison presence are new host state no PL term reads (OD-PL-33).]
- `P` — protection kept ÷ (kept + failed) over the fealty records the OBSERVER can know, in an `INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_year` window [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PL-r1-4: the landed keys are `one_week · one_month · one_season · one_year` (`intervalWeeks.js :: INTERVAL_WEEKS`)]; 0.5 when empty: the head reads all its own records; a member reads its own edge's record only; the omniscient preset reads them all. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-8: no observer reads a third court's record.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PL-V5 (RULINGS-ROUND3 A.6: "The hold's `P` moves by outcome; trust by answer"): `P` counts the OUTCOME rows only — `protection_kept` (the member stood) and `protection_failed` (it did not), §4.3 — never the liege's answer. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — RULINGS-ROUND4 A.6 · SEAMS-r3-5 (MAJOR: the den occasion wrote `protection_failed` only, so `P` could only fall by it): `P` counts the OUTCOME rows of EVERY protection occasion in BOTH directions — the war's siege and a menace's (§4.3), an incursion (§4.3 (3): by the contest where fought; by A.1's harm rule where never fought after a `sent`), the banner's cover of a levied member (§4.7 F.4) and THE SITES' den occasion, whose two closings are `protection_kept` (the den cleared within its clock — by the liege's answer or by anyone) and `protection_failed` (the clock run out with the den still holding — the liege answered and was repelled, or never answered), with NO row when the den ends or leaves by its own course (§4's sites bracket, item 2) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — `cure/D-ST-r4.md` §5's route (the r4 verifier's ROUTED-UNAPPLIED FAIL) · RULINGS-ROUND5 A.8: "the clock run out unanswered" let a REPELLED answer write no row, so `P` turned on the answer, against §4.9's "an answer never moves `P`"]; one outcome per edge per episode (§4.6).] The answer reaches the hold through `D̄` alone, and only as far as grip reads it: a `kept` answer's resentment is grip's term (`rulingPowerSeat.js :: vassalageSeat`: `- T.VASSAL_RESENTMENT * clamp01(num(Number(r.resentment), 0)) * (1 - clamp01(num(Number(r.fear), 0)))`), while a `sent` answer's trust is NOT a grip term (grip reads leverage, dependency, pact, fear and resentment) — trust steadies the oath through the vassal rule's stable compact instead (`relationshipRulesCore.js`: `const stableVassalage = clamp01(relState.trust * 0.34 + …`). Measured, `cure/D-PL-r3-probe/measure.out`.]
- `G` — share of direct vassals in a standing coalition against the head (0 while `coalitionLedgerEnabled` is dark).
- `X` — mean `1 − mappedDistanceWeight(head, member)` (0 aspatial).
- `A` — C8's term, THE AGE OF THE OATHS (dark until C8 THE AGES lands; then `hold01 −= 0.10·A`): the mean over the head's direct vassals, on the records the observer can know (`P`'s rule), of `clamp01(weeksSince(max(oathSince, the edge's newest surviving protection_kept)) / (25 × INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_year))` — the STANDING OATH's swearing (its first swearing or its last renewal, the summary field `oathSince` of §4.6, which a `fealty_renewed` moves and the cap never evicts) or the newest protection the member felt; a `fealty_sworn` row is NEVER read, because a baseline re-written after the cap's eviction or at a relight is bookkeeping, never a renewal; the old clause "an edge whose renewing rows the cap has evicted reads `1`" is STRUCK; and the term declares `politySuccessionEnabled` (the oath's own family, which writes `oathSince`) among its host flags, so with that family dark the hold is the six-term formula and `A` turns on no other family's flag [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — PL-X1 (MAJOR, EXECUTED by the r4 verifier, `skeptic/PL-r4-verify-probe/ageterm_r4b.out`: "an edge with 24 rows since its oath reads as freshly sworn" — a crown failing 1–10 rows a year read `A` 0.079–0.044, a defied crown 0.053, and the same failing edge with SUCCESSION dark 1.000) · RULINGS-ROUND5 A.7. Re-run on the r5 rule (`cure/D-PL-r5-probe/ageterm_r5.py`, an executor of these stated rules): the failing and the defied crowns read 1.000, a protective crown 0.039 (unchanged), a crown renewed at year 20 and then busy with failures 0.200, a relit world 1.000 (the oath kept through the dark span), and SUCCESSION dark leaves `A` dark]. Every constant DRAFT, UNMEASURED, on the FP-46 roster; C8's century soak measures it (CHAIR-NOTES 17 (d)). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — CHAIR-NOTES 17's PL half (the owner's word of 19:09: "kingdoms fall and are made"; the chair's (b): "no eternal capital (the crown's hold decays without renewal — PL5's hold read gains a long decay; apexes change)"). It is no second decay law (SP-B): nothing stored decays; the term is a derived AGE read from dated rows the record already holds, so a crown whose oaths are never renewed — no seat change asks its vassals (no mortality clock, CHAIR-NOTES 8), no protection is felt — loosens over a generation until its strongest member may press a claim (§5.2's occasion (2) reads `frayed`/`shaky`), and a crown that protects and is re-sworn does not. The other way, no runaway collapse: when a crown falls or breaks, the walk re-forms the chain around the next root by itself (PL1, PL2 — derived), and a re-founded town is sworn by the landed roads. The term reads only the OUTCOME `protection_kept`, never an answer (A.6's one path). Built and lit at C8's wave, a same-seed shift signed there; dark, the formula is the six terms above, byte-identical. Vetoable.]
Bands by `SEAT_TUNING.BANDS`' floors (0.70 · 0.45 · 0.20; one band derivation, the hegemony idiom): `unbroken ≥ 0.70 · staunch ≥ 0.45 · shaky ≥ 0.20 · frayed`. The hold has no decay law of its own; it moves only as its inputs move (C8's `A` is an input: the age of dated rows, never a stored decay).

**3.6 The kingdom's encirclement counterforce.** [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M11.] `hegemonyFear.js` (92 lines) reads `politiesOf` as a second source of `fear_of_dominance` beside the treaty sphere, with the same belief-side `strengthOf`: neighbours fear the kingdom they believe in (requirement 2). Behind `polityHoldEnabled` (§8); a same-seed shift wherever war reasons are lit, signed at PL-2's lighting. **Compromises — where the nuance lives** (each names the LANDED input it moves; closeness01 is moved by NONE of them — it is culture and trade). [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M6 · SX-17.]
- **Scutage** — coin in place of men (W-COIN's treasury, when lit): the summons is answered `answered` with coin; the member's BURDEN (protection-burden resentment) is spared; the crown's contingent is smaller.
- **Half service** — WC's `half_answered`; a `service_answered` row with the fraction.
- **Commuted dues** — one charter term trading the tribute rule's severity against the levy draw (two landed knobs).
- **The charter** — a TREATY between liege and member drafted by GRAMMAR's `PACT_DRAFT_LENS` on a `charter` occasion (a restive member; deference falling toward `present`) [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M14: fold §3.1.1 rules the lens as the producer; the chair amends its row to add the occasion; `peaceTerms.js` (799/800) takes it through a leaf car named in PL-5]. The three trade-rights rows (`toll_exemption · market_access · exclusivity`) are its terms; a charter enters grip's `pact` term, and so deference.
- **The autonomy charter** — a charter whose term LOWERS the liege's share by one band in named classes, never below `marginal`'s (one GRAMMAR term row, OD-PL-9). ⚠ Its code token avoids `autonom…` (`relationshipRuleHelpers.js :: ESCALATION_HINT` matches it, R4 H7): the term is `narrowed_voice` (free, 0 · 0 · 0). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-1: with no sphere left, the charter narrows the liege's VOICE; `narrowed_sphere` is renamed so that "sphere" names only hegemony's treaty arm.]
- **The court bond** — §15.6 (a slot, not built here).
- **Arbitration** — §6.

## §4 OBLIGATIONS IN DETAIL

**4.1 Service.** Today one hop, involuntary and measured silent. PL-5 (after WC-5) makes it a SUMMONS answered by each member on WC's `call_in_answer` (`answered · half_answered · refused · lapsed`): the fork reads the member's deference, its own war exhaustion, its own threat (an outside menace at its walls excuses it in the record's voice — "Kell kept its levy home: the Ashen Host is camped below its walls"), its strength, and its fealty record. Under `staunch`+ the summons CASCADES (the crown summons its direct vassals; each mid-lord answers for itself and summons its own); under `unbroken` the crown may summon every member directly — the hold read here is the crown's own, `polityHoldOf(…, crownId, crownId)` (§3.1 b). [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — STOP-PR-r4-1's PL half (RULINGS-ROUND4 §E: "PL §4.1 STANDS … a direct summons under `unbroken` carries the riders' line to the member, and the MID-LORD'S row is written either way — a credit on its service row when it lets its sub-vassal's riders go (F.6's lender's credit), a resentment patch when the crown rode past it unasked (F.6's precedence friction)") · SEAMS-r4-3 · RULINGS-ROUND5 A.8 — THE DIRECT SUMMONS AND ITS ROWS. (i) EVERY summons row is written on the answering member's OWN sworn edge, to its immediate liege, whoever called: the crown holds no sworn edge to a sub-vassal, so a directly summoned sub-vassal's `service_answered` / `service_refused` and its cover outcome (§4.7 F.4) sit on its oath to its own lord, episode the call-in request's key, and move that lord's hold exactly as in the cascade (PL5: the vassal of my vassal is not my vassal). (ii) THE MID-LORD'S ROW, written either way, ONE per lord standing between the crown and a member the crown summoned directly whose men answered (`answered` or `half_answered`), on that lord's own sworn edge: where the lord itself answered the same call `answered` or `half_answered`, it LET its vassal's men go — the lender's yearly credit (§4.7 F.6 (a): one `service_answered` row a year, episode `riders_lent:<rootId>:<yearStartTick>`, no patch); where the crown did not summon it in that call, or it answered `refused` or let the call lapse, the crown RODE PAST IT — the precedence friction, one `riders_quartered` row carrying ONE resentment patch (§4.7 F.6 (b), §4.9), episode the call-in request's key. Nothing is written for a lord between when the member refused (no men were taken past it). Draw-free, read from the call's own answers; P53 pins it. The verifier's three gaps (the mid-lord's row; an edge for a direct summons to write on; the ruled patch) are closed here and at F.6.] While a polity contest stands, an `aside` member answers by its fork with its allegiance as the excuse: a `service_refused` row carries no defiance price. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-18 · PL-r1-16.] Each answer writes a `service_answered` / `service_refused` row. The levy's silence is cured first (PL-5's car-0 measures why the release cases read zero). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — SEAMS-r2-10 (with PR-r2-17): THE RIDERS ARE INSIDE THE SUMMONS. A member's riders (THE PATROLS' detachments, `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_PR.md`) are drawn from the same one manpower truth this summons asks for (PR3: when the levy marches, the riders are absorbed into it), so the crown's call on them at war IS this summons, never a second one. The member's answer on `call_in_answer` carries ONE INPUT LINE for its riders, read by its fork beside its own threat (a den or a band pressing its roads is an excuse in the record's voice). `answered` sends them to the crown's war with its service (how they ride there is PR's). `half_answered` keeps them on its own roads, and the `service_answered` row's `fraction` says what stayed home. `refused` keeps all of them, and `lapsed` means the call ran out undecided. One fact keeps one hearing and one row (requirement 24; §4.3's relief precedent): no second registry kind hears the same men (PR's `patrol_order`, with its own `sent · kept`, is withdrawn from PL's side, §15.12), and no second `service_refused` row prices the same men for the same call. A summons row is PL's OWN. PL mints it, with `episodeId` the call-in request's own key (its `warAnchorKey` and `askedTick`, WC §1.9.1 — the request record carries no id field of its own), so there is one row per member per call; no other volume requests it, and it carries no request prefix (§4.6). Where PL-5's summons is not built (WC-5 unlanded) or `polityObligationsEnabled` is dark, the crown summons no member's riders at all; the root's OWN riders still ride members' roads by the hold (§15.12).] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — RULINGS-ROUND3 A.1 ("the levy draws riders, never a keep"), one clause for PL's summons: a HELD KEEP's garrison is the site's own complement, paid through its holder by the site's funding band and drawn from no town's manpower pool, so no summons calls it and no `service_answered` fraction counts it (THE SITES §4.1: "the keep neither marches nor is drawn").]

**4.2 Dues.** Unchanged (the landed rule), one hop, to every overlord edge (PL12).

**4.3 Protection — ONE relief decision with a refusal arm.** [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SX-07: wall relief was automatic and symmetric, the recall a probability-1 override, so "Merewick did not come" could never fire.] On a SWORN edge, relief is a DECIDED act `relief_answer: sent · kept` by the liege's own fork — deference owed (the seat band), its own threat, its exhaustion, and its BELIEF of the siege — DECIDED (the card's default) in the FIRST WEEK THAT RELIEVER BELIEVES the member's siege (or a menace at its walls, MN's besieger union) — the hop delay sets that week; in the omniscient arm it is the siege's first week — and MINTED as a `relief_sent` / `relief_kept` row keyed by the reliever and the siege episode when that week RESOLVES (THE MINT WAITS FOR THE WEEK, below) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — RULINGS-ROUND5 B.11 ("THE RELIEF WINDOW IS PER CANDIDATE … never 'week 1' absolute") · MN-X1's route to PL ("PL §4.3's 'the week … first appears' needs the same change"): measured, every rumor hop takes at least a tick (`rumorNetwork.js`: `const travelTicks = Math.max(1, finiteNumber(weeks, 1));`) and the rumor stage runs before every stage that asks (`cure/D-PL-r5-probe/measure-r5.out`), so "the week it first appears" asked a liege that could not yet believe and, the answer being taken once, never asked again. Before the reliever believes there is no answer, no line and no share. A reliever that never comes to believe before the siege ends was never a relief candidate, and its edge gets NO outcome row: a liege is never blamed for a siege it had not heard of (THE SITES §6.6's rule for a den) [REFINED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — the no-row case, so B.11 opens no unearned `protection_failed`; vetoable]]. The GATES follow the relief each arm really has. The two calls could disagree") · MN-r4-verify m6 ("two named callers"): `reliefAnswerOf` DECIDES for an actor, so it reads BELIEF (the one corpus law, CHAIR-NOTES 18 (d); truth only in the omniscient preset), and it reads ONLY the tick's SNAPSHOT — the seat band, the reliever's own threat, exhaustion and belief as the tick began, never the mid-tick worldState — so every caller in one tick gets the same answer: the war layer's own pass (`computeAllyRelief(snapshot, …)` already takes the snapshot), the polity stage's mint, MN's own siege leaf for a `menace_siege` (MN §4.7: the menace arm's caller is MN's leaf in `advanceCalamity`, not the polity stage) and PS's incursion leaf. With hearings dark the call and the mint therefore agree by construction; the carrier is the snapshot (PLAUSIBLE until PL-4's car-0 confirms that each input reads it)]; the generic symmetric ally relief stays automatic for unsworn allies until WC-4's contribution dispatch lands (then one road). Relief is counted in the siege verdict OR as a REINFORCE army, never both (MN-4 obeys the same rule). The failure window is the strike's hearing week, and the record keeps TWO FACTS of it: the ANSWER row (`relief_sent` / `relief_kept`), minted the week the answer RESOLVES (A.2), and the OUTCOME row, written when the siege ENDS — `protection_failed` if it ends `falls`, `protection_kept` if it ends any other way (`relieved`, `lifted`) — whatever the answer was; the receipt says which ("Merewick sent its soldiers, but Kell's walls fell" · "Merewick did not come; Kell held"). OF THE PROTECTION ROWS, ONLY THE ANSWER CARRIES A PATCH (elsewhere in the record only `renew`'s deference-up, §5.6, and every `fealty_defied` price, PL7 and §4.6, carry one): `sent` raises the member's trust in its liege and `kept` its resentment, through the one relationship writer as a state-only patch (§5.5 THE STATE). RULINGS-ROUND2 C.Q4 · PS14 — THE INCURSION ARM: an `armed_incursion` into a sworn member's land (THE PASSAGE §7.5: one STANDING card per territory crossed, each holder answering its own) is an occasion of this same decider under its WAR arm's gates, and the liege's duty AGGREGATES ON ITS SIDE: ONE answer per liege per incursion unit (the episode is the unit's `unitKey`, PS's STABLE unit key — §4.6's round-2b bracket), however many of its members' lands the unit crosses, written as an input line on each crossed member's card; the rows go to each crossed sworn edge under that one episode (`relief_sent` / `relief_kept`, then `protection_kept` / `protection_failed` when the incursion ends).] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — PL-V5 (MAJOR: on an incursion the liege's `sent` had no reader, yet wrote `protection_kept`) · RULINGS-ROUND3 A.6, a PS-and-PL JOINT ROW (PL owns the record and the answer; PS owns the defender term). (1) THE SHARE. A liege that answers `sent` adds its relief share to the DEFENDER's side of THE PASSAGE's ONE contest at the crossed member's border, whose defender is the member's FIELD FORCE (PS §7.5: "as the defender's size, B's DEFENCE `passageDefenceOf` (§2.7): its FIELD FORCE `passageFieldForceOf(B)` = `homeDefense − siteDefense` (A.1: the ready keep stays at the walls, ST §4.1 — never on the road at the border) PLUS, where B is a member whose crossing's LIEGE LINE reads `sent` as it stands at the resolution, the liege's relief share as `computeAllyRelief` computes it") [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — the r4 verifier's minor (§4.3 (1) called its quotation "re-cut to its current line", but PS now reads "B's DEFENCE `passageDefenceOf` … LIEGE LINE reads `sent` as it stands at the resolution"): re-cut again to PS l.375 as it stands at 2026-10-19; the substance agrees]. The share is the same per-ally term `computeAllyRelief` adds to a siege — `relief += (Number(capacityFor(ally)?.homeDefense) || 0) * ALLY_RELIEF_FRACTION;`, the landed fraction 0.4 that MN §4.7 exports once — read on the liege's FIELD FORCE, `homeDefense − siteDefense` (the liege's own keep never marches: ST10, "relief SUBTRACTS it"; with THE SITES dark `siteDefense` is nothing and the term is the landed one, byte-identical) — and it is nothing when the liege is under its own siege (`if (besiegedSet.has(ally)) continue;`). The same sum is what PS's threshold reads (A.1, PS's half): the crossing stands `barred` when the unit's size band is below `decisive` against the member's field force PLUS that share, else `unopposed`. The one answer per unit stands on every crossed member's card, so its share enters each of those members' contests and thresholds; it is counted in the contest or as a reinforcing army, never both (this section's siege rule).
