A site's defence, danger and readiness reach the polity through the one reads every decider already consumes (`homeDefense`, the danger ledger's presence term, `buildThreatByCid`), so any PL input drawn from them considers sites by construction (PLAUSIBLE until PL-4's car-0 names each relief input's read); the strength a member or a reliever brings to a BORDER or a RELIEF is its FIELD FORCE `homeDefense − siteDefense` — the ready keep stays at the walls (RULINGS-ROUND4 A.5; ST §4.1; PS §2.5) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — A.5]. Where PL must know a site AS a site, it does so by ST's named roads only: (1) a crown's fort in a member's land is the control instrument that OD-PL-33 moved to THE SITES, with resentment its counterforce; (2) a DEN pressing a sworn member is ST's `den_unanswered` OCCASION (ST §6.6), with TWO closings, both recorded through the request door above under the one episode `den_unanswered:<siteId>:<tick>` (registered in PL's own commit): `protection_kept` when the den is CLEARED within the occasion's clock — by the liege's answer (its sortie, its army, its riders) or by anyone, the member standing unharmed being the fact — and `protection_failed` when the clock runs out with the den still holding (answered and repelled, or never answered — the receipt says which: "Kell's lord came, but the robbers held the keep" · "Kell's lord never came"), and NO row when the den ends or leaves within the clock by its own course (a menace's end, a body's end or move: no protection was tested); the episode's `<tick>` is the occasion's OPENING tick — the first week the den presses the member at ST's threshold and the liege could know it (ST §6.6, OD-ST-46) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — `cure/D-ST-r4.md` §5's route to PL, verbatim in substance ("'when the clock runs out unanswered' → 'when the clock runs out with the den still holding (answered and repelled, or never answered …)', plus 'no row when the den ends or leaves by its own course'; the episode's `<tick>` is the occasion's opening tick") · RULINGS-ROUND5 A.8: under the old words a REPELLED answer wrote no row, so `P`'s outcome turned on the ANSWER, against §4.9 ("an answer never moves `P`"); re-checked against ST l.379 and OD-ST-46 as they stand]; so the hold's `P` moves BOTH ways by the den, like every protection outcome, with no patch on either row (§4.9), under the requester's flag ∧ `polityEnabled` (ST's `siteFatesEnabled`, §8's REQUESTED family) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — SEAMS-r3-5 (MAJOR: "the den occasion writes `protection_failed` and nothing ever writes `protection_kept` for a den the liege answers … `P` can only fall") · RULINGS-ROUND4 A.6 ("THE DEN OCCASION WRITES BOTH OUTCOMES"): ST supplies the two closings (ST's lane); because both are REQUESTED rows, the OBLIGATION flag's darkness no longer leaves the den one-way, which was SEAMS-r3-5's second half]; (3) a site GRANTED as a fief rides PL's charter occasion, and a site SEIZED in a polity war is named by `applyPolityVerdict`'s state pass — only after the verdict's relabels have landed (§5.5) — and both are applied by ST's one writer (`siteLedger.js`, whose closed caller list includes PL's charter and verdict leaves, ST §6.3).]

**4.7 THE BANNER — a member's riders under the crown** (RULINGS-ROUND3 §F; THE PATROLS own the riders' behaviour and write the section THE BANNER (PR11, §10.1 (c), §10.3), THE PASSAGE the crossing and its jurisdiction read (PS §5.5, THE BANNER arm; PS §14 its couplings row) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — the r4 verifier's minor: the jurisdiction read lives at PS §5.5]; PL owns the reads and the rows, written here). **The owner's words (2026-10-18 ~19:2x), verbatim:** "and i keep forgetting an important point, how do patrols work in a kingdom. I think patrols have multiple loyalties that allow flexibility but also closes a gap on things like civial war and such. Effectively, every patrol in a kingdom is working under the highest overlord banner, and every subsequent level until it reaches them inteh vassal chain with a home local font thet is their home settlement that they more or less take charge in and can with some flexibility operate in unity to cover the entirety of the kingdom region. This si also how we can express protection from the crown. so it blurs the line a bit between territories. What are your thoughts?" [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — RULINGS-ROUND4 A.7 · SEAMS-r3-6 (PL's half: "F.2's one share law and F.4's protection deed (the fealty record's rows)") · RULINGS-ROUND3 §F's PL side: F.1 cited, F.2 the row, F.3 the hold read, F.4 the rows, F.5 the break's effect on the stack, F.6 the grip and resentment counterforces.]
- **F.1 One home, a stack of banners.** A patrol has ONE home — the settlement that raises, pays and houses it, with first call on its own roads (THE PATROLS' envelope is the home's). Its banners are the home's chain as the walk reads it — §2's BANNER STACK, `polityOf(…, homeId).path` with `crownReachAt` at each rung, never stored — so when the chain changes, the banners change at the next read. A lord's call on riders reaches them only through the one summons, NEVER SKIPPING A LORD'S ROW: under every hold below `unbroken` the call goes rung by rung (each mid-lord answers for itself and summons its own, §4.1); under `unbroken` the crown may summon any member DIRECTLY (§4.1, the owner's precedence design), the riders' line going to that member, and every lord between still has its row — its credit when it lets the men go, the precedence friction when the crown rode past it (§4.1 (ii), F.6); the riders are an input line on `call_in_answer` (§4.1, OD-PL-50) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — STOP-PR-r4-1's PL half (RULINGS-ROUND4 §E: "§F.1's 'never skipping a lord' is AMENDED to 'never skipping a lord's ROW' … PL §4.7 F.1 is re-worded") · SEAMS-r4-3 · RULINGS-ROUND5 A.8: F.1's "rung by rung, never skipping a lord" stood against §4.1's direct summons under `unbroken`].
- **F.2 One share law.** The liege's share of a member's riders IS the share of its voice in every decision — PL4's `POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE[band]` of the member's seat weight toward its IMMEDIATE liege, at every tier — never a second share for riders. The crown's NET over the kingdom is THE PATROLS' one coverage composition over PL's reads: under the crown's own read `staunch`+ (§3.1 (e)) each member m LENDS `lend(m) = max(1, floor(n(m) × s(m) × hold01))` of its `n(m)` riders on the round, `s(m)` its band share toward its immediate liege and `hold01` the crown's own read — but only while it keeps at least THE HOME'S MINIMUM itself (`n(m) − lend(m) ≥` the minimum, DRAFT, UNMEASURED, PR's constant beside the per-stretch cap), else it lends none; a SUB-VASSAL lends through its LIEGE's line, up the chain rung by rung (F.1): its riders join the line its liege's banner carries, counted ONCE, never re-scaled at each rung and never computed at the crown's own share (`crownReachAt`'s paramount reach is the crown's voice in a sub-vassal's DECISIONS, PL5, not a call on its men); the net is Σ over members of `lend(m)` (plus the root's own riders, PR's), the per-stretch cap binding. PL owns the share, the floor rule and the hold; PR the composition, the minimum and the cap. [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — RULINGS-ROUND5 B.8 · PR-X1 (MAJOR, EXECUTED by the PR verifier: under `floor(n × share01 × hold01)` at the crown's `staunch` read no sub-vassal of any tier ever lent and a town-sized direct vassal lent one rider only at `strong`+, so the banner's lend, its credit and its `riders_lent` could never occur) · the r4 verifier's minor ("F.2 applies the hold twice to a sub-vassal: `share01` already carries the hold's reach, and the formula multiplies by `hold01` again"): the sum is recomputed as ruled and the sub-vassal's share is its own band toward its own liege — the hold enters once.]
- **F.3 The line blurs by the hold.** STRONG — the crown's own read `staunch` or `unbroken` (§3.1 (e)): the kingdom is one net, and the crown composes its members' riders across their borders. Each RECEIVING member decides at its own border, on its own belief of the hold (§3.1 (f)), whether a fellow member's riders ride its roads BY RIGHT OF THE BANNER (at `staunch`+: by right, no tolerance spent) or cross on THE PASSAGE's ladder as allies with the ally size tolerance (below). WEAK — the crown's own read `frayed` or `shaky`: each lord keeps his own roads, the crown composes no net, and its own riders keep to the root's land (§15.12). No new stance word: PS's jurisdiction read cites §3.1 (f).
- **F.4 The crown's protection is felt, and recorded as a deed.** THE OCCASION: a member answers its liege's summons `answered` WITH ITS RIDERS (their line sent, §4.1) while the crown's own read is `staunch`+ — the member's roads are now the net's to cover. Below `staunch` no occasion opens: each lord keeps his own roads, a crown that cannot hold the kingdom as one net promises no cover, and a weak crown's `P` is not drained by a cover it never offered. THE OUTCOME, ONE row on the member's OWN sworn edge (to its immediate liege, whoever's summons it answered — §4.1 (i)), episode the call-in request's key (the service row's own; PL's own row, the OBLIGATION family): read from THE BANNER's SEASON LEDGER — PR's per-member seasonal tally `weeksCovered`, the weeks of the season in which a rider of the banner not the member's own (the crown's or a fellow member's) stood PLACED (PR4's weekly placement) on at least one of its road halves, reset at the season — at the SEASON'S END: `protection_kept` when `weeksCovered > 0`, `protection_failed` when it is 0. Written once: at the end of the season in which the occasion stands, or at the member's riders' return home if that comes first (the tally so far); an occasion opened in a season's last month (DRAFT `INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_month`, UNMEASURED) is judged on the NEXT season's tally, so the net has weeks to ride; never from the one-week `lived` rows; no patch (§4.9) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — RULINGS-ROUND5 B.9 · PR-X2 (MAJOR: PR's `bannerCoverOf` read the placement digest's `lived` rows, one week deep, so a net that rode weeks 1–10 and then slipped wrote `protection_failed` at the season's end): PL reads the declared seasonal tally, PR's one-field addition (`weeksCovered`, PR's shape and lifecycle); the late-answer clause is REFINED, vetoable; the season boundary PL assumes is the tally's own reset, `tick − (tick mod INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_season)` (the `yearStartTick` idiom), routed to PR]. "Came to its aid" is never a number: the PLACEMENT is the deed and the count of riders never enters (PL-V5's principle). A body sees one banner — what it believes of the kingdom's riders deters it by what it has heard (THE PATROLS' believed watch; THE BODIES cite); PL writes nothing for it. Lit only where THE PATROLS are (`patrolsEnabled`) and PL-5's summons exists (WC-5): before then no riders are summoned (OD-PL-50) and no occasion opens.
- **F.5 Civil war, feud and break — the gap closed.** A BREAK re-points the breaker's link to `rival` (§5.5), so the walk no longer reaches its old liege: its patrols follow their home, and the crown's riders in its land are a FOREIGN UNIT — THE PASSAGE's rules apply to them as to any army, trespass by hostility included. A FEUD leaves every link sworn: the two members' patrols lose recognition by their pair's own war state (PR's friction), the stack above them unchanged. A POLITY WAR keeps every link sworn in storage, but while `swornLinkSuspended` holds, THE BANNER STACK is CUT at the suspended link (§2): a patrol follows its home's side, and the crown's riders in a claimant-side member's land are a foreign unit. Every banner reader — THE PATROLS' coverage composition and THE PASSAGE's jurisdiction read — reads the stack THROUGH the predicate and joins §5.3's GUARDED set (the walker reds a reader of the vassal type that does not). FIRST-VISIBLE (requirement 16): a patrol turned back at a border it rode freely the season before (THE PATROLS' and THE PASSAGE's line; PL-6's and PL-9's first sign wherever patrols ride).
- **F.6 Counterforces (PL's half).** (a) THE LENDER'S CREDIT: a member whose riders the net placed on a fellow member's road halves in a year — or a lord that LET its vassal's men go to the crown's direct summons under `unbroken` (§4.1 (ii)) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — STOP-PR-r4-1 · RULINGS-ROUND4 §E: "a credit on its service row when it lets its sub-vassal's riders go (F.6's lender's credit)"] — writes ONE `service_answered` row that year on its edge to its liege (episode `riders_lent:<rootId>:<yearStartTick>`, `yearStartTick = tick − (tick mod INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_year)`; PL's own row, the OBLIGATION family, no patch) — a credit the fealty question, the allegiance choice and the summons fork read. (b) THE PRECEDENCE FRICTION — a lord whose ground the crown took charge of over its head: ONE `riders_quartered` ROW on that lord's own sworn edge, carrying ONE resentment patch (the pair's resentment, a grip term, so its deference falls, §4.9) — PL's own row, the OBLIGATION family, never a weave incident: (i) THE SWEEP — riders of the banner not the lord's own PLACED on its road halves in a season in which no cover occasion stood for it (its own riders home, F.4), read at the season's end from the same `weeksCovered` tally (`> 0`), episode `riders_quartered:<rootId>:<seasonStartTick>` (`seasonStartTick = tick − (tick mod INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_season)`), at most one a season; (ii) THE RIDE PAST — §4.1 (ii), episode the call-in request's key, at most one per lord per call. Under a `frayed` or `shaky` crown no net is composed (F.3) and neither arm arises. [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — STOP-PR-r4-1's PL half · SEAMS-r4-3 · RULINGS-ROUND5 A.8 ("F.6 (b) prices the sweep as the RESENTMENT PATCH, not `riders_quartered` wear") · the r4 verifier's FAIL ("F.6 (b) prices an unasked sweep only as `riders_quartered` wear, and §15.9 says 'The weave is lit nowhere', so §4.9 has no patch for it"): the word THE PATROLS asked for (PR §7.6, OD-PR-26) is KEPT and RE-HOMED from §15.9's weave list to shape 3's closed kinds (§4.6), so the friction is priced where the weave is dark and never twice where it is lit (one path, §4.9); RULINGS-ROUND3 §F.6: "resents an overlord who sweeps his roads unasked (PL's precedence friction, resentment patch)". REFINED, vetoable: one kind for both arms of the friction, and the sweep read off F.4's own tally with the opposite occasion (a member whose men went to the levy is COVERED — protection; one whose men stayed home is SWEPT — friction).] (c) THE PEACETIME CALL: where the crown composes a member's riders onto another's roads in a season in which neither the root nor the lender is at war, the lend wears as `riders_lent`, ONE typed decaying incident per lender per season on its sworn edge (§15.9's closed list, registered in PL's own commit): resentment is a grip term, so deference falls along the chain the crown sweeps ("a crown that calls riders from every member in peace pays in resentment across the chain"); in war the lend is service and wears nothing. (d) The per-stretch cap stops stacking, and feuding members share no eyes (THE PATROLS').
- **F.7 The surface.** "The crown's riders keep the Tolle hills since Kell's riders went to the war." (the deed kept) · "No rider of the crown has come to the Tolle hills since Kell's riders went to the war." (failed) — §13. On scrutiny the patrol's card shows its home and its stack as the chain; the map draws the banner when the host lights patrols (the window, H9/H23 — never H16). "Banner" stays a PROSE word: PL mints no code token of that name (`banner` is landed 193 times in `src`, the UI word; `cure/D-PL-r4-probe/measure.out`).
[REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4), each vetoable — (i) F.4's occasion is the SUMMONS answered with the riders: measured, a campaigning town keeps at least its peace count of riders (PR3; PR §6, "the campaign count is NEVER below the court's own peace count"), and riders reach a liege's war only through PL-5's summons, so §F.4's "a member whose riders went to the levy" is, in PL's terms, that answer; (ii) no occasion below `staunch` (no downward ratchet on a crown too weak to offer a net); (iii) the lender's credit is a row a YEAR — a row a season would crowd the 24-row record and evict the oath rows the fealty question needs; (iv) `riders_lent` wears only in peace; (v) §F.7's sentence says "the crown's wardens", PL says "riders": SEAMS-r2-17 struck `warden` from PL (a landed role label, CH's display noun, the public-security OFFICE of THE BODIES' item 41), and the net is detachments, never named persons.]

**4.8 KEEPING THE PEACE — a body in a member's land** (THE BODIES `docs/DESIGN_FP_ARCH_BD.md` §7.1 and §10.2; their three routes to PL, `cure/L-BD.md` §4). **The owner's words (~19:1x), verbatim, the part that reaches the crown:** "settlemetns need to keep their own peace or be held responsible." Keeping the peace in one's own land is part of the member's SERVICE (PL8's "up"); the crown's part is its word and, under a firm hold, its ORDER to hunt; the price of defying that order is harbouring. Three pieces, one door — and, since round 5, the crown's faith and a foreign hand, (4) and (5) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — L-BD 23b · A.8]:
- **(1) `peace_unkept` — the member's default.** When a body based or born in a sworn member's land spills into a fellow member's or the liege's land (THE BODIES' spill, BD §10.2 (b), on BD's own clock and dwell — at most once per body per edge per dwell), THE BODIES REQUEST a `peace_unkept` row on the (liege ↔ member) edge through `requestFealtyRow`, episode `body:<kind>:<hash>:<tick>` (§4.6). It is the REQUESTED family's (§8): written under the requester's own flag ∧ `polityEnabled` — BD's `bodySpillEnabled`, whose liege face also needs `polityObligationsEnabled` (BD §13), so with obligations dark no row is requested. It carries NO PATCH (a default is a record, as `service_refused` is; §4.9): the pair's resentment moves once, by BD's peer face where the spill reaches the liege's own land (BD §10.2 (a)), never twice for one spill. Its readers are the fealty question, the allegiance choice and the summons fork (as every row on the edge) and the hunt order below; the hold's `P` never reads it (`P` is the liege's protection; this is the member's service). A body of any kind may be its subject — one cause family, the kind in the episode (THE BODIES' item 23b); a conclave's export is BD's to sign by faith and reaches PL as one of (4)'s three rows (a flock of the liege's own creed is SERVICE, never a default) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — L-BD 23b · RULINGS-ROUND5 A.8]. It is distinct from THE SITES' `den_unanswered` (the LIEGE's failure to clear a den pressing its member, a protection outcome): both may stand for one gang, one each way.
- **(2) THE HUNT ORDER — the liege's word.** In EVERY band the liege's own word toward a body (BD's `COURT_STANCES`, its derived default or its heard word) is one input of its member's `body_stance` fork at `POLITY_LIEGE_SHARE[band]` (PL4; §3.2's `justice_at_home` row). Under `staunch`+ by its own read (§3.1 (d), the arbitration's idiom) the liege may ORDER the hunt: `huntOrderOf(worldState, snapshot, { liegeId, memberId, bodyId })` → `{ ordered, reasons }`, pure and draw-free, `ordered` when the liege's own word toward the body is `hunted` (for a gang; toward a beast it is always `hunt`, BD10) AND an occasion stands — a `peace_unkept` row for that body on the edge within its dwell, or that body's raid on the liege's own land within BD's dwell — or the DM directs it (his line on the card). The order is ONE INPUT LINE on the member's `body_stance` card (BD's kind; PL mints no kind — one fact, one hearing, the relief precedent); its weight in the fork is the same band share (one share law); what the order adds is the PRICE of refusing it. It is lit under `polityObligationsEnabled` (the crown's peace, PL-7) with THE BODIES' `bodiesEnabled`. A paramount's order reaches a sub-vassal through its mid-lord's own `body_stance` (whose fork reads the paramount's order as its liege's), rung by rung, never skipping a lord. The crown's OWN riders sweeping a member's roads are THE PATROLS' `PATROL_ORDER` by the crown (PR's instrument), priced as the precedence friction's `riders_quartered` row (§4.7 F.6 (b)) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — A.8].
- **(3) HARBOURING A BODY — the price, never a break.** A member whose court keeps a gang `tolerated` (THE BODIES' derived `harboured`) while an ORDER of its liege to hunt that gang stands is harbouring the crown's enemy: THE BODIES request ONE `fealty_defied` row with its deference patch on the (liege ↔ member) edge through the door, episode `body:<kind>:<hash>:<orderTick>` (one per order), the REQUESTED family; it never opens `fealty_break` (PL7: the detector never reads the record). A beast is never harboured (item 23b: a court cannot harbour a pack — its neglect is `peace_unkept`); a conclave has no `harboured` word.
[CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — THE BODIES' three routes (`cure/L-BD.md` §4: "register `body:` in `FEALTY_EPISODE_PREFIXES` (harbouring); mint the OBLIGATION row `peace_unkept`; the crown's hunt order as a `body_stance` input"). BD calls `peace_unkept` an OBLIGATION row: it is an obligation by its MEANING (service) and REQUESTED by its DOOR, written under BD's flag with obligations as BD's own liege-face gate — the families' rule (RULINGS-ROUND3 A.5: "requested rows → the requester's flag ∧ `polityEnabled`"), so BD's §13 row and PL's §8 agree. BD §10.2 (b)'s "moving grip and resentment per PL §4.9" is answered by §4.9: the row carries no patch; grip moves by the refused order's price (3) and the pair by BD's own peer patch. Routed to BD.] [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — BD's route (1) prices "a gang the liege hunts"; PL prices it only where the liege has ORDERED the hunt. A lawful liege's DEFAULT word toward every gang is `hunted` (BD §7.1: "`tol ≤ −0.2` → `hunted`"), so pricing the stance alone would drain every tolerant member's deference by default, with no act behind it; the stance is the liege's voice in the fork, the order is the act whose refusal is priced — the shape of the crown's ban (CH's `polity_ban:` is priced only after the crown's act). BD's `harboured` for a NEIGHBOUR's hunt stays BD's own pair matter (no sworn edge, no fealty row). Routed to BD. Vetoable.]
- **(4) THE CROWN'S FAITH — a flock from a member's land** (THE BODIES §10.2 (b′); the owner's 18:28 word, L-BD 23b: "conclaves consequences following ideological differences"). THE BODIES sign a conclave's spread by faith (BD's one distance read) and REQUEST one of three rows through the door on the (liege ↔ member) edge, episode `body:<kind>:<hash>:<tick>` (BD composes it), each judged by THAT edge's liege's patron creed — every lord is the head of its own sub-tree (§3.1), so for a direct vassal of the capital it is the crown's own faith. (a) A flock of the LIEGE'S OWN creed spreading from the member into the liege's or a fellow member's land is SERVICE: ONE requested `service_answered` credit, no patch (§4.9), at most once per flock per edge per BD's dwell — read like every service row (the fealty question, the allegiance choice, the summons fork). (b) A flock of a RIVAL creed (BD's sign: far from the liege's patron) so spreading is DEFIANCE on PL17's conversion fault line ONLY where the member's court has ENDOWED it — `endowed` is the court's act, never a default (BD §7.1: "`endowed` is NEVER a default: it is the court's act") — ONE requested `fealty_defied` with its deference patch, never a break (PL7); a rival flock the court merely `permitted` (its own derived mind at home, `own_seat`'s ground), `curbed` or `outlawed` is the ordinary `peace_unkept` default when it spreads. The FAULT LINE itself needs no row: the rival creed's share in the member lowers `closeness01`'s faith term and so raises the break's `(1 − closeness01)` (§14.6), and a flip forced against the people is the landed `religious_conversion_fracture` (§14.3) — cited, never a second. (c) THE MIRROR COUNTERFORCE: a member whose court stands `curbed` or `outlawed` toward a flock of its liege's own creed in its land — ACTIVE stances (the court moves against the flock; BD §7.1: below `permitted` it is a public enemy, pursued) — pays ONE requested `fealty_defied` with its deference patch, episode the week that stance took hold toward that flock, never a break; "the crown's protection withdrawn one step" is that price's resentment falling through grip into the deference band the liege's relief fork reads (§4.3), never a protection row — an outcome needs its occasion and an answer moves no `P` (§4.9's one path). All three are the REQUESTED family's (§8), under BD's `bodySpillEnabled` ∧ `polityEnabled` with BD's liege face `polityObligationsEnabled` (BD §13); the hold's `P` reads none of them. [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — `cure/D-BD-r4.md` §5's route to PL (the r4 verifier's ROUTED-UNAPPLIED FAIL: "PL has 0 hits for flock, sponsor or curb, so the row BD cites ('the fealty row PL already names for it') does not exist") · RULINGS-ROUND5 A.8 ("a REQUESTED `service_answered` under `body:` for a flock of the crown's creed; a rival creed's spread as defiance on PL17's line; the cost of curbing the liege's creed") — the door already returns rows of any closed kind (`cure/D-PL-r4.md` open item 1), so no kind and no prefix is added. REFINED, vetoable: (i) each edge judged by its own liege's creed (§3.1's idiom), routed to BD for its "the capital's patron"; (ii) the rival creed's defiance priced only on the court's own act of endowment — round 4's rule for harbouring (an act, never a stance by default, §4.8 (3)); (iii) the mirror priced on the active stances, and its "protection step" carried by deference, not a second input on the relief fork, which would count one defiance twice.]
- **(5) A FOREIGN HAND IN A MEMBER'S LAND — the crown affronted** (THE BODIES §10.5 THE SPHERE: "sponsoring a body in a rival crown's MEMBER affronts the CROWN through THE BANNER's protection … the crown's grievance, not only the member's"). When a sponsorship of a body in a sworn member's land LEAKS (BD's `sponsor_exposed`, §10.4) and the sponsor is no member of the member's kingdom, the affront reaches the CROWN as well as the member: the crown is the member's paramount by the walk (`polityOf(worldState, snapshot, memberId).rootId`, the banner stack uncut — a suspended link cuts it, §4.7 F.5), and it is affronted only while its own read of its hold is `staunch` or firmer (§3.1 (e)): the crown that holds the kingdom as one net is the one whose protection the sponsor defied, while under a weaker crown each lord answers for his own land (§4.7 F.3). PL writes NO row and mints no kind: the affront rides BD's one leak — the crown takes the same casus on the landed grievance ladder against the sponsor that the host court takes (BD §10.4 (ii); never a second ladder) — and PL supplies which crown and whether it holds. A sponsor INSIDE the kingdom is a fellow member: its hand in a member's land is a feud matter, the crown the first mediator (§6), never a second affront. [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — `cure/D-BD-r4.md` §5 (the fourth L-BD 23b item) · RULINGS-ROUND5 A.8 ("the affront of sponsoring a body in a rival crown's member"); the `staunch` bound and the inside-sponsor case are REFINED, vetoable; routed to BD.]

**4.9 WHAT MOVES THE OATH — the patch, the outcome, grip and the hold, once.** [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — the PL-r3-verify minor ("E.2 and fold l.683 cite 'PL §4.9', which does not exist"): RULINGS-ROUND3 §E.2 ("PL §4.9's wording governs"), the fold's `fealty_*` ↳ r3 row ("grip moves by resentment, never by trust (PL §4.9)") and THE BODIES §10.2 (b) cite this number; the passage they meant is written here from PL8, §3.5's `P` bracket and §4.3, so the citations have their target and nothing is renumbered. The "deference patch" of PL7 and §5.6 is stated as the pair's RESENTMENT (up for a defiance, down for `renew`) — the one grip term a state patch moves. Vetoable.]
1. **Rows that carry a PATCH** (state-only, through the one relationship writer, §5.5 THE STATE): the protection ANSWER — `relief_sent` (trust up) and `relief_kept` (resentment up); `renew`'s deference-up (resentment down, §5.6); and every `fealty_defied` price — PL7's two acts, a refused arbitration order (§6), every REQUESTED harbouring (CH's `polity_ban:`, PS's unit prefixes, BD's `body:`) and THE BODIES' two faith defiances (§4.8 (4): a rival creed endowed; the liege's creed curbed) — resentment up; and the PRECEDENCE FRICTION, `riders_quartered` (§4.7 F.6 (b): the crown's sweep of a lord's roads unasked, its direct summons riding past a lord) — resentment up [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — A.8: the ruled patch has its row]. EVERY OTHER ROW carries none: the OUTCOMES (`protection_kept` · `protection_failed`, of every occasion), the service rows (`service_answered` · `service_refused`, the banner's credit, the mid-lord's credit and THE BODIES' crown's-faith credit included), `peace_unkept`, the baseline `fealty_sworn` and the `fealty_withheld` rows. `requestFealtyRow` returns a patch only for `fealty_defied`.
2. **GRIP** — the owner's scalar `weight01`, so the member's deference and the liege's share — moves by RESENTMENT (`rulingPowerSeat.js :: vassalageSeat`: `- T.VASSAL_RESENTMENT * clamp01(num(Number(r.resentment), 0)) * (1 - clamp01(num(Number(r.fear), 0)))`), by the pact term (a charter), by leverage, dependency and fear — NEVER by trust. Trust steadies the vassal rule's stable compact (`relationshipRulesCore.js`: `const stableVassalage = clamp01(relState.trust * 0.34 + …`), the oath's other side.
3. **THE HOLD** moves only by its terms (§3.5): `D̄` (grip's mean, so a `kept` answer's resentment and every defiance price reach it), `P` (OUTCOMES only, both ways, of every protection occasion), `L`, `Sᵦ`, `G`, `X`, and C8's age of the oaths `A`.
4. **WEAR** — §15.9's typed decaying incidents (`riders_lent` and the instruments') feed resentment, hence grip, where the weave is lit; `riders_quartered` is a ROW with its patch since round 5, never also wear (one path) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — A.8].
One path per quantity: an answer never moves `P`, an outcome never moves grip, and nothing moves trust but an answer's patch — there is no second loyalty law (PL8).

---

## §5 THE POLITY WAR — CIVIL WAR, THOUGHT THROUGH

[CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-m1: renamed from "crown war" in every code token; prose says "civil war" and "the crown".]

**5.1 Is it still the same kingdom? Yes.** Every sworn link stays sworn IN STORAGE; the polity read returns ONE polity for the contest's whole life; the dossier says "the crown of Merewick is contested by Brenmoor"; outsiders still treat with "Merewick and its sworn towns". What changes is that the members are on SIDES, and the sides fight. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PL-r1-6: this now holds through a conquest of the crown too. A claimant holding the crown's seat at the matured rung makes `foreignSeatOf` a two-cycle (executed, `cure/D-PL-r2-probe/cycle.rerun.out`), which the walk reads as the crown in chains (PL2): root H, class `occupied`, one polity; P33 pins it.]

**5.2 The claim.** A CLAIM to the crown is pressed by a SWORN member's court (never an outsider — that is conquest) in a polity of THREE OR MORE members (a pair's claim is a break, PL9). Occasions: (1) a member answers the fealty question `claim` (§5.6); (2) a strong member against a weak crown — PL's own decider in the polity stage, weekly: the hold `frayed` or `shaky` as the member believes it, the member's believed strength share at or above the paramount's, its legitimacy above the paramount's [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SX-12: occasion (2) is decoupled from GR-4's host, so the claim and the sides are preset-blind and aspatial; born vassals sit two tiers down, so this occasion is rare on born polities — PL-10 measures claims per century before C9]; (3) the DM's `OPEN_CLAIM` (§9), which opens the claim already `pressed` [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-30: the engine's default never withdraws a claim the DM opened ("We only supply setting and consequence")]. The claim's strength reads `CLAIM_BASIS_BY_RULING_POWER` (`titularSuccession.js`), dark until heirs light; the aspatial arm reads strength share, legitimacy and a court bond with the paramount when bonds land. The claim is the `polity_claim` hearing (`pressed · withdrawn`).

**5.3 Sides, and the suspended link.** Each sworn member answers `allegiance_choice` (`crown · claimant · aside`) — the contested lord stands on `crown` and the claimant on `claimant` by construction, and a member is `aside` until it answers [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-16] — reading its deference to the paramount against its closeness to the claimant (the owner's three), its BELIEF of the two sides' strength, its fealty record, and its own appetite (SP-C); a mid-lord's answer carries its vassals' default, which they may answer differently. [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-B4 · SX-20, decision side.] ONE shared leaf predicate `swornLinkSuspended` (§2): while true, the sworn edge READS AS `rival` to every war and peace decider — a legal war target (`HOSTILE_TYPES` includes `rival`) and no longer a friend. Nothing is relabeled; the christening and the walk stand. Each reader takes the predicate as a one-line guard behind `polityContestEnabled`. [REFINED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — measured (`vassalReaders.txt`, 52 files name the vassal type): the ruling's nine readers are joined by five more war-side readers, and a WALKER pins the set.]

The walker `tests/lint/swornReaders.walker.test.js` lists every `src/domain` file that reads the vassal type OR one of its three channel types and REDS unless it is in GUARDED (above) ∪ EXEMPT (with a reason: the display and news voices — `newsVoice.js`, the two rumor phrase pools, `envoyNews.js`, `lineageNews.js`, `sovereigntyNews.js`, `warCoalitionNews.js`, `warRulingsNews.js`, `wizardNews.js` — narrate only; the canonical alias table; genesis; the bundle's writer `region/graph.js`; the seat read itself, a structure read whose two decision consumers are GUARDED) — a new reader cannot forget the predicate (structural prevention; shrink-only exemptions). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-5: the walker keyed on the vassal TYPE could not see channel-type readers; it now walks both.]

**5.4 The persisted fact — `spatialLedgers.polityContests` (persisted shape 2 of 4).** `polityContests[contestedLiegeId] = { contestId, claimantId, crownId, sinceTick, sides: { [memberId]: 'crown' | 'claimant' | 'aside' }, causedBy, pendingVerdict? }` (`sides` always holds the crown as `crown` and the claimant as `claimant`; `pendingVerdict: { verdict, tick, causedBy }` is the one-tick carrier of a verdict decided while hearings are dark, read by the pre-fold verdict leaf and cleared by the polity stage — §5.5) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-16 · PL-r1-2] — one record per contested lord (a contest may form at any tier), drop-when-empty, closed vocabulary, ONE writer (`polity.js :: writePolityContest`, called by the polity stage at a `pressed` verdict and by `applyPolityVerdict`). It is a DECISION, so it is state (W-SEAT). A TRACKED/EXEMPT row in `spatialUsage.js` (EXEMPT: not a mover). [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-m7: PL-9's stage is the polity stage; its writer is named.]

**5.5 Endings — the verdict, its channel and its default.** [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M1.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-2 (RULINGS-ROUND1 PL-r1-2) · PL-r1-4 · PL-r1-6 · PL-r1-20: the writer no longer relabels — a relabel in this engine is four writes plus the cascade inside frozen `applyWorldPulse.js`, and `applyPulseMover` drops any graph the polity stage's host returns; every verdict now has a default condition read from the field; conquest across a suspended link is ruled; the chair's STOP-1 ruling (A′) is carried with (B) as its named fallback.]

**THE CHANNEL** (measured, §0). `applyPolityVerdict` NEVER relabels. A verdict that re-points links is minted as TYPED RELATIONSHIP OUTCOMES in the shape `occupation.js :: vassalizationOutcomes` already uses — `{ type: 'relationship', candidateType: 'polity_verdict', ruleId: 'polity_verdict', applyMode: 'auto', probability: 1, relationshipKey, relationshipPatch: { proposedRelationshipType, trajectory: 'transitioning', overlordSaveId?, vassalSaveId? }, proposalPayload: { kind: 'relationship_label_change', relationshipKey, fromType, toType, reason }, metadata: { fromType, toType, relationshipSeed?, polityVerdict: { contestId?, verdict, causedBy } } }` — by `src/domain/worldPulse/polityVerdict.js :: polityVerdictOutcomes`, called ONCE from the kernel's pre-fold verdict host `coup.js :: coupVerdictOutcomes` (126 lines; zero kernel lines). The outcomes enter the ONE outcome fold that tick, and the frozen applier writes the label, the channel bundle, the neighbour networks and the cascade exactly as it does for every relabel. A re-pointed link with no pair edge carries `metadata.relationshipSeed` (materialised by `ensureRelationshipEdgeSeed`, "not a second relationship writer"); a new liege is stamped explicitly (an explicit re-stamp on a `toType: 'vassal'` write wins). **AUTHORITY** [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PL-V2: the one minor row let an ENGINE DEFAULT (a crown passing, a partition, a vassalage formed or broken) pass the DM's majors pause on a month advance, while the landed `occupation_vassalized` — the same structural shape — meets it (`decisionTier.js`: "classifies a selected outcome on STRUCTURAL markers, never severity or applyMode … a vassalage"; fold 24(c): the landed majors pause and its DM switch are UNTOUCHED, S5-B3)]: `changeAuthorityPolicy.js` gains TWO rows, both `{ authority: 'auto', module: 'polityVerdict.js', consultsProposalFlag: false }` (its contract test reads the mint from `src/domain/worldPulse/`, which is why the leaf lives there): `polity_verdict`, the candidateType of a SEALED verdict (the DM gave it on the card), and `polity_verdict_default`, the candidateType of the ENGINE's default (a docketed default the DM left, or a verdict decided while hearings are dark), which also carries `campaignAltering: true` and joins `decisionTier.js :: CAMPAIGN_ALTERING_CANDIDATE_TYPES` (the contract test's `CAMPAIGN_ALTERING` list grows by one). The hearing seal IS the DM's gate (requirement 24: never a second gate), so every mint sets `applyMode: 'auto'` directly and is NOT routed through `authorityFor` — the `occupation_vassalized` precedent — and a SEALED verdict is minor: the majors pause never asks the DM twice about his own word (the one exception, a seat appointment's `government_change` payload, is major by the landed payload rule, §15.4). A DEFAULT is a major: where the DM keeps his majors pause, the advance withholds it and stops at its tick exactly as for `occupation_vassalized` (`pulseOutcomePartition.js :: partitionPulseOutcomeLanes`: `const deferredMajors = deferMajors ? publicSelectedOutcomes.filter(outcome => deriveDecisionTier(outcome) === 'major')`), and his answer at the pause is the verdict. [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — RULINGS-ROUND1 PL-r1-2 ruled "ONE cause row making `polity_verdict` auto-apply"; two rows, both auto, keep that ruling's substance (auto-apply, never a proposal, the seal as the gate) and differ only in the majors marker — vetoable.] **THE SOURCES** the pre-fold leaf reads, in order: (1) a held PL docket row, where `hearingWeekEnabled` is lit — the verdict is the hearing seal, else the default stored at docketing (requirement 24: "the NEXT advance resolves every docketed hearing FIRST"); (2) a verdict decided LAST tick while hearings were dark — the contest record's `pendingVerdict` (§5.4), or a fealty row whose `answer` re-points (`free`, `submit`; §4.6). Source (1) with a seal mints `polity_verdict`; source (1) on its default and every source (2) mint `polity_verdict_default` (PL-V2). It mints only where the edge still carries the old label, so it is idempotent by state. **THE STATE:** in the same tick, AFTER the fold, the polity stage (`applyPolityVerdict`) writes what is not a label — the contest record (re-pointed, dropped, or its `pendingVerdict` cleared) through its one writer; the fealty rows and deference patches through `applyRelationshipPatch` as state-only patches with no `proposalPayload` (the `sovereigntyTransfer.js` precedent); the recall stamps below; and the receipt, whose `causedBy` names the seal event (or the docket row where the default ran). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PL-V2, NO GHOST STATE: for a verdict that re-points links, the state pass writes ONLY where the post-fold graph its host already receives (`advanceTreatiesWithDisposition`'s `graph: applied.regionalGraph`) shows that the verdict's relabels landed. A default withheld at the DM's majors pause, or set aside by him there, leaves the contest record, its `pendingVerdict`, the fealty rows and the deployments exactly as they were, and the default re-reads the field the next week (a standing engagement's default is read every week, fold 24(a)); so under his pause a default he sets aside is offered again while its condition holds, until he seals a verdict on the card. A verdict that re-points nothing (`lapsed`, a `crown_prevails` that only re-swears, `continues`) writes its state as before.]

**THE RELABEL ORDER** (pinned by P20 and P21): (1) every link that leaves the old lord relabels to `neutral` (the edge keeps its relationship record; only its label and its seniority stamps clear — "a transition away from vassal/patron clears them"); (2) the old paramount's own new edge (`vassal`, the new lord stamped as overlord); (3) each re-pointed member's edge to the new lord (`vassal`, seeded if absent). The vassal formations run LAST, so each formation cascade (`resolveRelationshipHierarchy`, NOT suppressed — the correct one-shot alignment of a re-sworn link) runs on the final shape.

**THE MEMBER WARS** — the chair's STOP-1 ruling (A′), RULINGS-AFTER-COMPILE. While a link is suspended the PEACE SUIT ON IT IS REFUSED (`warPeaceDecision.js` is a guarded reader, §5.3); the sides fight until the contest ends; `polity_war` is STANDING, so the DM may end it any week (the owner's "force the hand"). At EVERY verdict that ends the contest (`claim_prevails · crown_prevails · partition · lapsed`) the writer closes the member wars on the contest's fronts through the canonical recall writer, `warIntent.js :: stampDeploymentRecall(state, attackerId, targetId, 'polity_verdict', tick)`, on every live deployment between the two sides (the `warRulingsEvidence.js :: applyVerdictWarDissolutions` precedent, a verdict recalling a front). The war layer resolves each as a withdrawal at its next tick ("a deliberate recall / negotiated peace is a CHOICE, not a defeat: NO disposition delta"), and `polity_verdict` is registered in `concludedWarRecord.js :: CLOSE_ROADS` (thirteen tokens become fourteen; "An unmapped cause reds the discovery arm"). Frozen `warTermination.js` takes zero lines — CONFIRMED by code read; the end-to-end closure is PLAUSIBLE until PL-9's car-0 executes it. **FALLBACK (B)**, named by the chair: if that execution finds a war that cannot close this way, a flagged PEACE_STEP polity arm ends it in a `truce` mark on the contest record that writes no label.

**CONQUEST ACROSS A SUSPENDED LINK.** Occupation of one side's town by the other is the contest's EVIDENCE — it feeds the default conditions below — never a relabel of its own: `occupation.js` is a GUARDED reader (§5.3), so `vassalizationOutcomes` skips an occupier↔occupied pair of contest parties on a suspended link (the verdict writer re-swears it), and a conquest of a contest party opens NO `polity_fallen` (§5.7) — the standing `polity_war` is the one hearing for that fact. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PL-r1-6: the round-1 guard kept the edge but not the walk. The matured RUNG itself makes the two-cycle — executed: `foreignSeatOf(H)` names M by `occupation_rung_vassalized` and `foreignSeatOf(M)` names H by `vassal_edge` — and the old guard dropped the polity as a `polity_circle` in the middle of the contest. The walk now reads that unbacked rung step as the crown in chains (PL2): H stays the root, class `occupied`, one polity (P33); the held seat is evidence for `claim_prevails`.]

**THE VERDICTS** of `polity_war` (`continues · claim_prevails · crown_prevails · partition · lapsed`) [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-m2 · SX-20: `lapsed` added] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PL-r1-4: `INTERVAL_WEEKS.season` and `.year` do not exist — `intervalWeeks.js :: INTERVAL_WEEKS` is `{ one_week: 1, one_month: 4, one_season: 13, one_year: 52 }`, the keys MN already cites — so, read literally, both windows were `undefined`, neither `claim_prevails` nor `partition` could ever default, and the contest ran `continues`; the real keys are written], each with the DEFAULT CONDITION the engine reads from the field (truth-side, like every executing writer; checked in this order, and the first that holds is the default; every window DRAFT, UNMEASURED):
- **`crown_prevails`** — default when the CLAIMANT is held (occupied by a crown-side town) or no claimant-side town stands unheld. The claimant's side re-swears (links kept; a deference-down patch) or is stripped (conquered towns held; a town the crown casts off goes free).
- **`claim_prevails`** — default when the claimant's side has held the contested lord's seat for a season (`INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_season`). The claimant becomes the paramount: every link to the old paramount re-points to the claimant (each direct vassal then answers the fealty question, `renew · free`), and the old paramount answers its own. The card OFFERS the DM the christening's re-key (PL3).
- **`partition`** — default after a year (`INTERVAL_WEEKS.one_year`) in which each side has held its own towns and neither has held the other leader's seat. The claimant's sworn side re-points to the claimant (the claimant's own link to the old paramount relabels `rival` through the same channel): two polities. The name stays with the side holding the old paramount; the other is unnamed until the DM names it. The chronicle records the lineage ("Brenmoor's towns broke from the crown of Merewick").
- **`lapsed`** — default when neither side has fought for a season (no live deployment between the sides) AND neither side holds a town of the other's leader; UNREACHABLE while either side holds the other leader's seat. The contest record drops; the links un-suspend.
- **`continues`** — the default every week none of the above holds.

**STOP-1 — RULED (A′) by the chair** (RULINGS-AFTER-COMPILE §PL), carried above as THE MEMBER WARS with (B) as its named fallback. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-20 and the L-FOLD lane's unplaced item: the STOP text that left (A)/(B) to the chair is replaced by the ruling.]

**5.6 THE FEALTY QUESTION — PL's own decider.** [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M2 · SX-14.] [REFINED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — measured (`cure/L-PL.md` §1e): no single landed record carries every seat change; the ruling's "rulingPowerHistory" does not exist.] PRODUCERS, both LIVE, read through ONE key [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-9: keying rows by position and label failed — `MAX_PREVIOUS_GOVERNMENTS = 6` and `.slice(-MAX_PREVIOUS_GOVERNMENTS)` shift every position, `claim` and `free` wrote no row, the 24-row record could evict the guard while the 6-row history kept the producer, and lighting found every historic row unanswered]: (1) a ruling-power transfer on the lord's `powerStructure.previousGovernments` (`transferRulingPower`: coup verdicts, conquest, appointment, and the DM's `CHANGE_RULING_POWER`, whose row carries no tick); (2) a governing-seat change in the lord's `seatTransitions` (coup · challenge · succession · vacancy · government_change; each row carries a stable `id`). Both move the lord's EPISODE KEY (§4.6: `authorityTransferEpochFor(lord)` joined to its newest `seatTransitions` id). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — PL-V3: the key is `seatEpisodeKeyOf`, and this question is the KEY FORM of the one shared read `seatChangedSince` (§2) — the same read CH's pardon, PT's repudiation and GR's successors consume.] A direct vassal is asked exactly when the lord's current episode key differs from the `episodeId` on the newest epoch-bearing row of that vassal's edge; an edge with no such row receives the `fealty_sworn` baseline and is not asked. [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — RULINGS-ROUND3 A.5 asks the dark-after-lit path per family (§10); for this family it needs one more clause, here. The difference must also be FRESH: the lord's newest row in either tail is stamped at this tick or the previous one (`seatChangedSince` in its tick form, `since = tick − 2`), or the transfer tail's newest row is tick-less (only the DM's own `CHANGE_RULING_POWER` writes one, §2). A STALE difference — `politySuccessionEnabled` relit after a dark span, or a lord whose change the record never met — is BASELINED (a `fealty_sworn` row at the lord's current key — a BOOKKEEPING row that never moves the oath's `oathSince`, §4.6 [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — PL-X1 · A.7]) and never asked: that seat change's climax week passed unheard, and requirement 24 queues nothing. The fallen lord's `polity_fallen` (§5.7) obeys the same clause: a stale conquest row opens no question. P14, P15 and P39 are unchanged; P46 pins the relight. The one residue is a tick-less DM change made while the flag was dark, which is still asked at relighting; it is the DM's own act, and his card may dismiss it. Vetoable.] **STOP-2 — RULED (RULINGS-AFTER-COMPILE §PL): the gap is ACCEPTED AND RECORDED.** A DM KILL/ASSIGN moves neither tail, so it opens no question by itself; the DM's own KILL/ASSIGN verb card offers "ask the vassals" — one act that opens the fealty question at the next week; GR's six-site succession record is GR's inbox row (fold §3.1.10), the slot. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-20 and the L-FOLD lane's unplaced item.] Each DIRECT vassal of the lord answers `fealty_question` at the next one-week advance, and EVERY answer leaves its keyed row (§4.6): `renew` (a `fealty_renewed` row and a small deference-up patch) · `withhold` (the link stands; a `fealty_withheld` row; dues suspended one season — a priced sulk; the liege may arbitrate or make war) · `claim` (opens `polity_claim`, in a polity of three or more; else it is `free`) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — the PL-r2-verify minor (with succession lit and the contest dark, a `claim` had nowhere to go) · RULINGS-ROUND3 A.5: `claim` is OFFERED ONLY while `polityContestEnabled` is lit; dark, it is absent from the decider's choice set and from the card, whose answers are then `renew · withhold · free`; P41 extends] · `free` (a relabel to `rival` through the polity verdict channel, §5.5 — never the landed `labelProposal`, whose always-proposal gate would be a second DM gate for one heard fact [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-10]). ONE HEARING PER PAIR: where GR-4's `succession_reaffirmation` would fire for a treaty between the same pair, the fealty question subsumes it and the treaty question is re-asked only after `renew`. The decider reads the vassal's deference, its fealty record, its belief of the new lord's strength and legitimacy, and the cause (a coup's heir is weighed harder than a succession's).

**5.7 The fallen lord.** When a paramount or mid-lord falls to conquest (`previousGovernments` row with cause `conquest`), the walk stops at it (PL2) and each direct vassal answers `polity_fallen` at the next week: `submit` (its edge re-points to the conqueror through the verdict channel, §5.5) · `stand_by` (keeps its edge to the held lord's court) · `free` (a relabel to `rival` through the same channel). Default: `stand_by` where the vassal's deference band is `strong`+, else `free`. A conquest of a polity-CONTEST party opens no `polity_fallen`: the standing `polity_war` is that fact's one hearing (§5.5). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-6 · PL-r1-2.]

**5.8 Feud, break and polity war, side by side.**

| | Feud | Break (secession) | Polity war (civil war) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Who fights | two members | a member against its liege | members against members, over the crown |
| At stake | their own quarrel | the member's freedom | who holds the crown |
| Is the polity at war? | no | no — it loses a member if the break holds | yes, with itself; still one polity |
| The links | stay sworn | the breaker's link → `rival` | stay sworn in storage; read `rival` across sides while suspended |
| The crown's role | the first mediator; may order it ended under `staunch`+ | the party broken from; may make war to bring it back | a side |
| Ends in | arbitration, exhaustion, a peace | the break held (free) or quashed | `claim_prevails · crown_prevails · partition · lapsed` |
| Smallest polity | three (two members) | two | three |

## §6 THE CROWN'S PEACE — FEUDS AND ARBITRATION

Co-vassals of one liege may be hostile with no rule today (the cascade excludes `vassal`). PL-7 makes the common liege THE FIRST MEDIATOR: the one mediator finder (`peaceTermsGraph.js :: findCrossPressuredMediator`, 78 lines, "THE ONE FINDER, never a second algorithm") gains ONE precedence rule — a common liege of both parties is tried first, cross-pressured or not, in EVERY band. Under `staunch`+ — the liege's own read of its hold, `polityHoldOf(…, liegeId, liegeId)` (§3.1 d) — the liege may ORDER the feud ended: `polity_arbitration` (`accept · refuse`); refusal is priced (a `fealty_defied` row and the deference patch — the OBLIGATION family's row, §8 [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3) — A.5's families]) and, under `unbroken`, is cause for the liege's war of submission. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-26 · PL-r1-18: one rule for §3.2 and §6 (the sphere table's "`frayed`: tolerated" is gone with the sphere); head and observer named.] [CURED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — SPL-M12 · SX-13: the finder's precedence rule and the war of submission sit under `polityObligationsEnabled`; the finder is lit in dramatic and full, so lighting is a declared same-seed shift there.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — THE BODIES' route (3): the crown's peace has a second face, the king's peace against a body in a member's land — the liege's word in the member's `body_stance` fork in every band and its ORDER to hunt under `staunch`+ by the same own read (§4.8); unlike a refused feud order, a refused hunt order is priced as harbouring only and is never cause for the war of submission (bounded: macro over micro).]

At a one-week advance each climax is docketed and the DM may leave it, TILT it (a bounded thumb, slight · heavy: a declared term on the decider's score for every PL decider, which draw no randomness, and a DRAW BIAS on `fealty_break`, whose landed default is a roll) or give a VERDICT [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-23: requirement 24(b) "a draw bias where it does; each kind declares which"]; month, season and year advances run defaults (requirement 24(c)); nothing is held over an advance. A polity war's verdict in any week forces the hand that week. No PL kind involves the party, so no party default applies.

**PL's input LINES on other volumes' cards — none is a kind** [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — PL-W1 · RULINGS-ROUND4 A.2 · THE BODIES' route (3): one place that lists them]: the RELIEF line on the `siege` card (the war's), MN's `menace_siege` card and THE PASSAGE's `armed_incursion` card (the liege's `sent · kept`, §4.3 — one line per liege per unit, mirrored); the RIDERS' line on WC's `call_in_answer` (§4.1); the HUNT ORDER's line on THE BODIES' `body_stance` (§4.8). Each is that card's input like any other (the DM may leave, tilt or set it), each has its DEFAULT from a pure, draw-free PL decider (`reliefAnswerOf`, the summons fork, `huntOrderOf`), and each row it causes is MINTED when the card's week RESOLVES, from the line as it stands — THE MINT WAITS FOR THE WEEK (§4.3 (2)).

| REQUESTED | any row another volume asks through `requestFealtyRow` (§4.6): CH's harbouring (`polity_ban:`), PS's harbouring (`army:` · `column:` · `patrol:` · `corps:`), ST's `den_unanswered` (`protection_kept` or `protection_failed`, its two closings), THE BODIES' `peace_unkept` and harbouring (`body:`) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — A.6 · THE BODIES' routes (1)(2)], and THE BODIES' three faith rows (§4.8 (4): the liege's-creed `service_answered` credit, the endowed rival creed's `fealty_defied`, the mirror's `fealty_defied`) [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — L-BD 23b · A.8] | the REQUESTER's own flag ∧ `polityEnabled` (ST's `siteFatesEnabled`; THE BODIES' `bodySpillEnabled` for `peace_unkept` and the three faith rows, whose liege face also needs `polityObligationsEnabled` by BD's own gate, and `bodiesEnabled` — the court stances' flag — for harbouring, which also needs a hunt order standing, so `polityObligationsEnabled` with it) | nothing at that requester's door |

So, with `politySuccessionEnabled` lit and `polityObligationsEnabled` dark (a legal combination under OD-PL-14), the fealty question writes its own baseline and rows and a `free` decided with hearings dark keeps its carrier (P38, P41). With `polityBreakEnabled` lit alone, PL7's price is written and a `succeeds` carries itself. The readers are unchanged and read whatever rows exist: the hold's `P` reads the protection rows (OBLIGATION and REQUESTED), PL7's definitional default reads this war's relief and protection rows (while OBLIGATION is dark only a REQUESTED `protection_failed` can stand there; with none, the default is `succeeds`), the fealty question reads its edge's newest epoch-bearing row, and the pre-fold leaf reads the re-pointing rows (SUCCESSION and BREAK). Two occasions that A.5 does not name are placed by the same rule, marked [REFINED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r3), vetoable]: (1) a refused arbitration order's `fealty_defied` belongs to OBLIGATION, because the order is PL-7's under that flag (§6), so lighting the crown's peace never needs the break's flag; (2) a `fealty_break` verdict's `fealty_withheld` row belongs to BREAK, because the break mints it, so the break never writes the SUCCESSION family's output. The shared parts — the shape's normalizer in `ensureRelationshipState` and the one append in `applyRelationshipPatch` — are built by the first family's wave to land (the shared-leaf idiom) and carry no flag of their own; a record holding only one family's rows normalizes the same. P46 pins every family lit alone.

**A contest outlives its flag** [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-17: (A′) closed member wars only through the verdict writer, which never ran once its flag went dark, leaving wars on links that read `vassal` again — `PEACE_STEP` has no `vassal` row, and "A vassal/patron hierarchy edge is never a besiege candidate"]. With `polityContestEnabled` dark and a `polityContests` record present, the polity stage runs only its WIND-DOWN arm at the next advance: every such contest closes `lapsed` — recall stamps with cause `polity_verdict` on every live deployment between its sides, the record dropped, one receipt per contest ("the contest for the crown of Merewick was set aside"). It is the landed wind-down idiom (`concludedWars.js :: closeRoadFor`'s Road 7: "the war layer was switched off mid-campaign and resolved everything"). With no record present the stage is a no-op (byte-identical); P32 pins both.

Alignment: recognition and appointment read the liege ruler's lawfulness and the member's (a lawful court withholds from a usurper). `causedBy`: every legitimacy delta names the recognition event; an appointment names the DM's act and the seal.

## §13 WHAT A DM READS (felt sentences under the Evidence Law — deeds only, never forecasts; the surface shows the outcome, the WHY is on scrutiny)

- "Ashford is sworn to Merewick; Merewick answers to no one." (surface: the dossier line)
- "Kell kept its levy home: the Ashen Host is camped below its walls." (surface)
- "Merewick did not come when Brenmoor's host besieged Kell." (surface; the scrutiny walk names the siege and the answer) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-30: the Evidence Law's "name who" — the besieger is named]

(surface — an `unopposed` crossing's outcome, by its harm after either answer: a `raid` upon Kell that `succeeds` or is `bought_off` is the harm; the threshold and the answer on scrutiny) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4) — A.1 · PL-V5] [CURED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5) — RULINGS-ROUND5 A.6 · the PL-r4-S1 re-ruling]

Every sentence names who, upon whom, and what happened; none says what might happen; no numerals.

## §14 CONVERSION — RITES, FAITH AND THE FORM OF GOVERNMENT THROUGH THE CHAIN (wave PL-11)

**The owner's question (2026-10-17), verbatim:** "how does the conversion of culture and relligion happen in kindgom through chains of vassalage"

**The answer.** Culture in this engine has no stored value to convert: it is DERIVED from faith, alignment, economy, the form of government and shared rites (`cultureDistance.js`: "NEVER a new authored coordinate"). So a kingdom converts its towns when their RITES, their FAITH and their GOVERNMENT drift toward the crown's — slowly, a link at a time, a year at a time — and the town's closeness to its liege (inside the owner's scalar) rises as they do. [REFINED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — R4-2: no culture writer is minted.]

**14.1 What already happens (R4, CONFIRMED).** Rites: one trade per CONQUEST-made vassalage on a year-keyed draw, restored when the vassalage ends. Faith: the vassal edge carries faith both ways and the overlord's `political_authority` channel carries the liege's creed down, distance-weighted — but only where `faithSpreadEnabled` is lit (dramatic, full). Closeness already reads faith axes, rites and government.

**14.2 Rites down sworn links (the one rite writer).** `traditions/relations.js :: applyImposition`'s trigger widens from `vassalOverlordOf` (the `vassalized` rung) to the LIEGE named by the walk's one step, passed in by `traditionsKernel.js` (493; `advanceRelations` receives only worldState, so the kernel passes the liege map in — one call into a leaf). The chance on a sworn link is `IMPOSE_CHANCE × weight01` (conquest keeps the flat 0.3) [REFINED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — R4-1: scaled by the LINK, not the crown's hold], diluted by `mappedDistanceWeight` (aspatial 1), drawn on the landed YEAR-keyed fork; one trade per liege per link (the landed `suppressedBy` idempotency). ⚠ `applyRestoration` widens WITH the trigger (R4 H1: it reads `vassalOverlordOf`, so a sworn-link imposition would be restored the next tick). Tier-by-tier lag is emergent: a mid-lord whose top rite is an imposed copy passes it down a year or more later (PLAUSIBLE; `buildImposedCopy` caps scale at the local band — PL-10 measures it). Kinds reuse the landed `imposition` (`mutationLog`) and `rite_imposed` (incident); never `crown_conversion`.

**14.3 The crown's faith works on courts first.** [REFINED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — R4-3.] The immediate liege counts inside `religionLegitimacy.js :: neighbourEndorsement` weighted by its `weight01` (the landed word `endorsement`; no new weight in the sum-one target; host 227), so the liege's creed raises its own LEGITIMACY in the member (the court's acceptance) and never touches the SHARE step (the people move only by the contest's own forces). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-12: "sampled once per year … slow by construction" is struck — `religionLegitimacy.js :: neighbourEndorsement` reads every neighbour's patron every tick (the liege already counts at 1/N through `neighbourIdsOf`), and `stepDeityLegitimacy` closes `LAG: 0.12` of the gap a tick, so a once-a-year term would be forgotten within weeks, and a held yearly sample would be an undeclared persisted shape.] The liege REPLACES its 1/N share of the endorsement mean with a `weight01` share — `endorse = weight01 × liegeTerm + (1 − weight01) × meanOfTheOthers`, under the landed `PREVALENCE_CAP`, a declared blend inside the one function (DRAFT, UNMEASURED) — read every tick like every neighbour. The court's acceptance is therefore the FAST echo (weeks); the SLOWNESS lives where it already lives: the patron flip waits on the people's share lead, which the liege never touches. Spread-gated by the landed code (`const neighbourIds = spread ? neighbourIdsOf(snapshot, cid) : []`): in the default preset the crown's faith does nothing — reaching it is an OWNER LIGHTING ACT recorded as a slot (OD-PL-25). The patron flip, when it comes, is the landed `religious_contest_conversion`; a flip forced against the people is the landed `religious_conversion_fracture`. Deity doctrine: the followers act, the god does not. ⚠ Closeness compares deity AXES, not identity (R4 H4): no felt sentence may say "they share the crown's god" from closeness — the sentence reads the patron itself.

**14.4 Government converges** through §15.4 (appointment) and §15.5 (the crown-backed faction), which `cultureDistance`'s government term then reads.

**14.5 Missionary access by charter** — a `grant`-executor term row read by `faithCarriersOut` as a carrier: NOT built in PL-11 (the catalog row needs a leaf car out of `peaceTerms.js` 799); recorded as a slot in GRAMMAR's catalog.

**14.6 The fault line.** [REFINED 2026-10-17 (fp-final) — R4-5.] The break's `independencePressure` reads `(1 − closeness01)` ONCE (`relationshipRulesCore.js :: vassalRules`, 744 → one leaf call; DRAFT coefficient 0.12, UNMEASURED): a town of a different faith, rite or government strains its oath more — "Kell keeps the old gods; its oath to Merewick frays" becomes backable. The polity war's `allegiance_choice` reads closeness to each side (§5.3).

**14.7 Champions as accelerant** — a saint or a heretic works on a creed through `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_CH.md` §6.2's THREE faith carriers: (3a) the ENTRY carrier — CH's leaf `paragonFaithCarriers(worldState)` notes an exerting saint's creed as reaching its town inside the spread lane's carrier-reach build, so a creed with no foothold can ENTER through `religionState.js :: attemptEntry` (`religiousContest.js`, step "3a. entries — faiths reaching C not yet present": `const entered = attemptEntry(state, deity, strength, { force: Boolean(occupied), tick: stampTick });`), spread-gated with that lane (`faithSpreadEnabled`); (3b) the run-side multiplicand on `religiousContest.js :: advanceReligionStates`' `strengthByRef` (CH's `paragonFaithMult`); and the birth-side `religious_conversion_fracture` row of `stressorGates.js :: STRESSOR_SPAWN_GATES` — a pointer, nothing built here. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r2) — D-CH-r2 §5.1 (CH-r1-9's third carrier, routed to PL): "two faith carriers" → three; the 3a entry carrier is CH's.] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — SEAMS-r1-24: `clergyDrag` is a function-local in `religionLegitimacy.js` (`let clergyDrag = 0;`, clergy taint) and `chronicleMomentum` reads `pulseHistory` — neither is a seat for a paragon.]

**14.8 The clocks (requirement 9).** Rites: yearly draw, one trade per link (years). The liege's endorsement: every tick, weighted by `weight01`; legitimacy follows at the landed `LAG 0.12`/tick (the fast echo, weeks); the patron flip after the landed share lead (the slow verdict, months to years — the people move only by the contest's own forces). The fault line: weekly with the vassal rules (it reads a slow quantity). FIRST-VISIBLE: the court's honour line the week its legitimacy band moves (the faith arm, month-felt); the planting line the year of a rite draw (the rite arm, built behind PL-1 and PL-4 — §9 PL-11). [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-12 · PL-r1-13.]

**14.9 Counterforces.** Imposition raises resentment (the landed `rite_imposed` grievance; weave-gated, lit nowhere — lit, it lowers grip and so deference); the fault line raises the break; a break restores the suppressed rite (the landed restoration); distance dilutes.

**14.10 Same-seed shifts.** Behind `polityConversionEnabled` everything is dark-identical; lit, the rite arm shifts the four presets where `traditionsEnabled` is lit wherever a sworn vassal exists (64–86% of realms), and the faith arm shifts dramatic and full — declared, signed at PL-11's lighting.

## §15 CONTROL AND COUNTERFORCES — HOW THE CROWN KEEPS ITS VASSALS IN LINE (wave PL-12)

**The owner's words (2026-10-17), verbatim:** "as well as government structure, voice in the court, etc... Essentially, the ways in which the crown and higher order vassals try to keep their lower order vassals in line nad limit coups and rebellions. But of course there are counterforces as well. And these conversions that happen are slow."

**The answer.** Every instrument runs through a writer that already exists; each acts fast and WEARS (a typed decaying incident on the sworn edge — one decay law); great lords use the same instruments down their own chains (every instrument is pairwise); the crown reaches past them only through PL5's blend, as far as its hold carries. The hold (§3.5) takes their results: protection in `P`, leagues in `G`, and resentment from every instrument's wear inside `D̄`; recognition works on the MEMBER's legitimacy (its coup and rebellion gates), never on the hold. [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-21.]

| Instrument | The existing writer / seam | Clock · wear | Counterforce (landed read) | Gate · presets |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **15.1 Recognition** | a one-off act at a member's seat change: `polity_recognition` (`recognized · withheld`) → `generosityUpdates.js :: applyLegitimacyDeltasToUpdates` on the member's `publicLegitimacy.score`, which already feeds the coup's `govMultiplier` and the ≥75 floor of the rebellion and insurgency gates — zero new terms [REFINED — R4-4]; a legacy bare-number legitimacy is skipped by the applicator, and the receipt says so (P28) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-22] | once per seat change; the delta decays with legitimacy's own law | a usurper withheld from rallies the commons (`stressorGates.js :: rebellionGate`); standing withdrawal is SEAT-8's typed stake | `polityControlEnabled`; all six presets |
| **15.2 Backing in a coup** | `convergence.js :: scorePreserveOrder` (a liege backs its member's incumbent; LIVE in war presets). ⛔ The coup budget is FULL: "The five bounds sum to exactly 0.815 against a DECLARED_BUDGET of 0.82" — no new coup term. A liege can never back a challenger in its own member (landed). The zeroed `kinship` motive carries shared faith/culture (R4-10, OD-PL-30) | the intervention's own life | OUTSIDE PATRONS court the restive: `install_friendlier_regime` admits a non-hostile edge when the member's link is strained — never the liege or a lord on the member's own chain (R4-11, OD-PL-29, PL-r1-27; `convergence.js` 764 → a leaf call) | `interventionEnabled && warLayerEnabled` (dramatic, full) |
| **15.3 A voice at the court** | down: the ONE `liegeStanceToward` read in the member's book (§3.3); up: the weighted mean of the great vassals' stances toward a counterparty as ONE bounded signal (DRAFT ±0.1) in the crown's declaration, through the chooser's shared-signal car — no fifth book [REFINED — R4-7] | per decision | the great vassals' voice IS the counterforce at the crown's council | `polityPrecedenceEnabled` (owner-lit seat) |
| **15.4 Appointment (the hard end)** | PRODUCER: the DM's `APPOINT_TO_SEAT` act (liege · member · the member's faction to seat) — the engine never opens an appointment; the `polity_appointment` hearing asks the member in its week (default its fork: `appointed` only at `dominant` deference); `appointed` changes the seat through the landed `government_change` payload with cause `appointment` (seed 45, its own stability line; the applier applies it for any applied outcome — `applyWorldPulse.js`: `if (FACTION_PAYLOAD_KINDS.has(outcome.proposalPayload?.kind))`), minted through the polity verdict channel under the seal, never the domestic challenge's always-proposal road (`applyWorldPulseFactionRoster.js :: applyFactionPayloadEffect`: "The challenging faction takes the seat", classed `faction_government_challenge`). `decisionTier.js :: isGovernmentChange` classes every `government_change` payload major, so where the DM keeps realm directive 7's majors pause the sealed appointment also waits on it — the DM's own standing option (S5-B3: untouched), said on the card. `puppet_seat` executes nothing [REFINED — R4-8] [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — PL-r1-19: the hearing had no producer and would have been followed by the challenge's proposal] | instant on the verdict; wears as the appointee's legitimacy seeds low | local legitimacy against appointees (seed 45, `oldPull 0.10`); the deposed house keeps its place and its grudge (`transferRulingPower`) | `polityControlEnabled` |
| **15.5 Shaping government** | one bounded tilt (OD-PL-31) in the lit faction competition toward the member faction whose government preference mirrors the liege's ruling power; a leaf-first car out of `factionCompetition.js` (775) | seasonal | rival factions' own power bases; the internal party of independence (`settlementPolitics.js` `break_vassalage`, dark) | `factionCompetitionEnabled` (five presets) |
| **15.6 Bonds · spies · a resident envoy · garrisons** | NOT BUILT — slots (OD-PL-33): court bonds (`court_match`, `fosterage`, `hostage_exchange`) a GRAMMAR unit; spies along sworn links ES's inbox; a resident envoy the errand spine's inbox; VASSAL GARRISONS new capability — THE SITES volume's (a crown's fort in a member's land, held by the crown's garrison; the chair's 10-18 ruling moved the slot from WC's inbox) [CURED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1) — HOST-r1-15] | — | — | — |
